- **Data flow notes:** `loadAlerts()` → shared sets → per-event `hasAlert`; read-only.
- **Testing notes:** No `WeekGrid2D` rendered inside the day; an alerted event shows ⏰; the shared helper returns the same result the week/month use; no dead imports remain.
- **Performance notes:** Removing the strip drops a whole second week render from each day view — a net win.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Identical signal logic.
- **Integration points:** §6.5, §7.x (alerts), 4.3.6 shared alert helper.
- **Status:** **Partial** (no-op strip present; no alert icon on day blocks). **Next:** delete the strip + dead imports; mark alerts via the shared helper.

## 4.4.9 — Bus integration + render guard + virtual scrolling (shared migration; emit `dayFocused`)

- **Purpose:** Put the day on the Phase 2 `EventBus` via the **shared** `Calendar2D` migration (4.1.9/4.2.9/4.3.9), coalesce re-renders, and meet the §16.6 virtual-scroll target — adding the day-specific `dayFocused` emit and retiring the `window` events.
- **Dependencies:** Phase 2 (EventBus), Phase 3.1 (view nav, `modeChanged`), Phase 3.3 (`preferenceChanged` for the hour-range), the shared migration done once in `Calendar2D` (4.3.9); 4.4.6, 4.4.7.
- **Acceptance criteria:** `Calendar2D` subscribes to `navigateTo` (incl. `level:"day"`), the timeline-changed bus event (re-render when in 2D), `modeChanged` (render only when `2d`), and `preferenceChanged` (the `weekHourRange` axis preference shared with the week); it emits `dayFocused {date}` on entering/navigating a day (§13.2) and `navigateTo {level:"day"}` from drills; `dayFocused`'s subscriber is `InklingPanel` (§13.2). The `window` `timelineUpdated` / `inkling:alerts-updated` listeners (Calendar2D.js:383–388) are migrated to the bus (shared with the week).
- **Implementation notes:** **No new subscription plumbing** — the bus migration is the same once-in-`Calendar2D` change from 4.3.9; the day only adds the `dayFocused` emit in `openDay` / day-nav (4.4.6). **Virtual scrolling (§16.6 line 1489, explicitly Day + Week):** only build blocks within the visible scroll window of the axis; recycle on scroll — pairs with the 4.3.3 axis and the 4.4.6 persisted scroll position. Apply state before emitting; coalesce back-to-back model/nav/preference events into one re-render via the shared render guard.
- **Edge cases:** Events arriving while in 3D (keep state, skip render); duplicate `navigateTo`/Today for the already-active day (no-op, no spurious `dayFocused`); drilling week→day must not double-emit; the now-line timer (4.4.4) keeps ticking independent of the render guard.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Invisible plumbing; payoff is top-bar "Day"/"Today" and Inkling "go to tomorrow" now move the 2D day view, and Inkling receives `dayFocused` to summarize the day.
- **Data flow notes:** bus `navigateTo {level:"day"}` → Calendar2D state → render `DayView2D`; day entry → emit `dayFocused` → InklingPanel.
- **Testing notes:** `navigateTo {level:"day"}` shows the day; timeline/preference change re-renders only in 2D; entering a day emits `dayFocused` once; no reliance on the `window` events remains; off-screen time rows aren't in the DOM.
- **Performance notes:** Subscribe once on mount (shared); render guard coalesces; virtual scroll bounds DOM to the visible window.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Identical plumbing; swipe nav (4.4.6) and axis scroll (§ line 1746) ride existing handlers.
- **Integration points:** §13.2 (`navigateTo`, `dayFocused`, `modeChanged`, `preferenceChanged`), §16.6 (virtual scroll), §2.1.
- **Status:** **Missing** (`window` events; emits nothing; no virtual scroll). **Next:** ride the shared bus migration, add the `dayFocused` emit + render guard + virtual scroll.

## 4.4.10 — Cross-check with §6.5, §6.1, §16.5/§16.6, §17.5, §13.2, §11 — close out Phase 4

- **Purpose:** Confirm the day view honors the 2D-view contract, the shared time-axis/overlap/virtual-scroll rules, and the event catalog — and, as the **last milestone of Phase 4**, verify the four sub-views (year/month/week/day) form one coherent drill on one data source and one bus.
- **Dependencies:** 4.4.1–4.4.9 (and, by extension, 4.1–4.3).
- **Acceptance criteria:** Verified against §6.5 (full-width vertical time axis, hourly-label margin, now-line on today; blocks = full width, left accent, full title, time range, body preview, priority; tap → Detail, empty slot → New Event, swipe ←/→, day nav), §16.5 (overlap sub-columns, 80px min), §16.6 (virtual scrolling — Day **and** Week), §17.5 (desktop drag/resize — model-gated, documented), §6.1 (shared data source; sub-view switch never changes `calendarMode`), §13.2 (`navigateTo`/`dayFocused`/`modeChanged`/`preferenceChanged`), §11 naming (`DayView2D`). The dead week strip is **removed**; the year→month→week→day **back-chain and drill are coherent**; deviations fixed or **documented** (the start/end + all-day + body + priority model adoption, and the deferred drag/resize).
- **Implementation notes:** Walk the reconciliation list and confirm each gap is closed or documented: full-width axis, positioned blocks (interim-vs-full model), left-accent rich content, now-line, overlap (reused helper), day nav + Today + swipe + scroll persistence, split tap routing, strip removal + shared alert icon, bus + `dayFocused` + virtual scroll. **Phase-4 close-out:** confirm a sub-view switch (year↔month↔week↔day) never writes `calendarMode` (§6.1); no view performs event-store writes (§2.3); the bus migration is genuinely **once** in `Calendar2D` (not duplicated per view); the shared axis/overlap/alert helpers are each single implementations; and the hour-range + week-start preferences are consistent across week and day. Compile the cross-milestone follow-ups (model adoption of `startTime`/`endTime`/`allDay`/`body`/`priority` per spec §2; drag/resize; legacy `WeekView` overlay retirement) into one Phase-1/follow-up list.
- **Edge cases:** Re-assert read-only; same-day/Today `navigateTo` no double-emit; day crossing month/year correct; DST + now-line documented; out-of-range now/events clamped.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Confirm "immediate visual response" (§1.4) on day nav, Today, now-line tick, block tap, and empty-slot create.
- **Data flow notes:** Confirms the day's bus sources/subscribers match §13.2 and it shares the model with year/month/week.
- **Testing notes:** Spec-to-implementation checklist passes for §6.5/§6.1/§16.5/§16.6/§17.5/§13.2; the year→month→week→day drill + back-chain is verified end-to-end on one data source; no `calendarMode` writes from sub-view changes; the consolidated model-extension follow-up is recorded.
- **Performance notes:** Confirm the render guard + virtual scroll hold under back-to-back model/nav/preference events across all four sub-views.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Both layouts verified at §8.7 breakpoints, including overlap and swipe nav; drag/resize desktop-only and documented as model-gated.
- **Integration points:** §6.1, §6.5, §16.5, §16.6, §17.5, §13.2, §11 — and the whole §6 2D-view family.
- **Status:** **Checklist** (gates the milestone **and** Phase 4). **Next:** run after 4.4.1–4.4.9; record the consolidated model + drag/resize + overlay-retirement follow-up list.

---

### Milestone 4.4 — Definition of Done
- `DayView2D` is upgraded from a flat list to the §6.5 single-day timeline: the **same vertical time axis as the Week View, full width** (one shared axis component, not a second implementation), with a side margin of hourly labels, reading only from `timelineModel.js` (§2.3), sitting correctly in the year→month→week→day back-chain.
- Events render as **full-width positioned blocks** (start→end, no-end → 30-min, all-day row when the model allows) with the §6.5 rich content — **left-border category accent, full (untruncated) title, time range, ≤80-char body preview, priority indicator** — and **overlap split into ≥80px side-by-side sub-columns** via the reused 4.3.5 helper.
- A red **current-time "now" line** marks today at the current minute (ticking), absent on other days, with scroll-to-now on initial open of today.
- **Day navigation** (prev/next day), a **Today** button, keyboard ◀/▶, and **swipe ←/→** all work and emit; the **time-axis scroll position persists across day-to-day navigation within the session** (§ line 1783).
- Tap routing is split per §6.5 — event block → Event Detail (§6.6, or documented fallback), empty slot → New Event prefilled with date+time; the dead no-op `WeekGrid2D` strip is **removed** and alert icons come from the **shared** alerts helper.
- The day is driven by the **EventBus** — riding the shared once-in-`Calendar2D` migration (subscribing to `navigateTo {level:"day"}`/`modeChanged`/`preferenceChanged` + the timeline-changed event, emitting `dayFocused`, §13.2) — with a render guard and **virtual scrolling** of the time axis (§16.6, explicitly Day + Week), replacing the legacy `window` events.
- Switching the 2D sub-view never changes `calendarMode` (§6.1); the view performs no event-store writes; the hour-range/week-start preferences stay consistent with the week.
- **Documented carry-forwards (consolidated for Phase 4):** the live event model never adopted the spec §2 datetime model — shipped on the interim "start + 30 min, all timed" positioning with **no** body/priority data; the real adoption of `startTime`/`endTime`/`allDay`/`body`/`priority` is one Phase-1 model task that also unblocks **desktop drag-to-move and resize** (§17.5) in both Week and Day; the legacy `WeekView` overlay retirement (from 4.3) is in the same follow-up list.
- **Phase 4 (Calendar 2D Core) complete:** year, month, week, and day all render from one data source on one bus through one shared axis/overlap/alert/preference layer, with the coherent year→month→week→day drill and a single consolidated model-extension follow-up list.


---

<!-- source: phase-05-milestone-5.1-scene-init.md -->

# Phase 5 — Milestone 5.1: Scene Initialization (Expanded)

**Goal:** Establish a single, canonical, correctly-parameterized Three.js scene bootstrap for WordWeaver per §5.2 — `WebGLRenderer` (antialias, pixel-ratio capped at 2), a `PerspectiveCamera` sized for the year overview, a defined lighting rig, a WebGL-support guard with graceful 2D fallback, one persistent animation loop, resize handling, and bus-driven lifecycle — while reconciling the **two independent scenes that already exist** (`src/scene.js`'s viewport scene driving `CalendarApp`, and `WordWeaverScene` with its own canvas/renderer/loop) and the **duplicate event-bus** the 3D stack runs on. This milestone is scene *plumbing only*; the year layout it kicks off (`createYearLayout`) is detailed in 5.2.
**Spec alignment:** §5.1 (3D world powered by Three.js, rendered in a `<canvas>` that fills the viewport behind the UI shell; **scene persists in memory as long as the app is open — not destroyed when switching to 2D**, lines 476–478), §5.2 (Scene Initialization: the 9-step sequence — `WebGLRenderer` antialias+alpha, `pixelRatio = min(dpr, 2)`, `PerspectiveCamera` FOV 60 / near 0.1 / far 1000, year-overview camera offset, Ambient 0.4 + Directional 0.8 + Hemisphere 0.3, `createYearLayout()`, start RAF, resize handler — **and the WebGL fallback: detect support, else set `calendarMode="2d"`, one-time notice, skip 3D**, lines 480–496), §2.3 (Rendering Systems: Three.js r152 minimum, scene root `WordWeaverScene.js`, `WebGLRenderer` antialias, pixel ratio capped at 2.0, lines 143–148), §3 line 77 (mode transitions animate: 3D→2D fades the Three.js canvas out / 2D→3D reverses, 400 ms ease; **both systems stay instantiated in memory, only visibility changes**), §11 line 1005 (architecture tree: `WordWeaverScene` is "3D, **always mounted, hidden in 2D mode**", over a Three.js Canvas), §13.2 (event catalog: `initialized {eventCount}`, `eventCreated/eventUpdated/eventDeleted`, `starterDataCleared` are consumed by `WordWeaverScene`; it emits `dayFocused/weekFocused/monthFocused`, lines 1160–1169), line 436 (`navigateTo` dispatched via the event bus, listened for by **both** `WordWeaverScene.js` (3D) and `Calendar2D.js` (2D)), §2.3 read-only contract (3D scene reads event data exclusively from the timeline model).
**Sequencing:** First milestone of Phase 5 and the foundation the rest stand on — 5.2 (year layout) is literally step 7 of the §5.2 sequence and cannot be specified until scene/camera/lighting ownership is settled here; 5.3 (DayBlock3D) and 5.4 (AtomGlyph3D) populate the scene this milestone creates; 5.5 (camera) deepens the camera/controls stubbed here; 5.6 (interaction) builds on the raycaster/pointer plumbing this scene already wires. Like Phase 4, every task is **grounded against existing code** (reconcile built / gap / next-step, not greenfield) — and Phase 5 inherits Phase 4's recurring finding that the calendar surfaces run on `window` events / a side bus rather than the §13.2 Phase-2 bus, now sharper because the 3D stack ships a **literal second `EventBus.js`**.

---

## Current implementation status (reconciliation)

The single biggest fact for all of Phase 5: **the spec's "one `WordWeaverScene.js` that fills the viewport and is the 3D scene" does not exist as described. There are two separate Three.js scenes**, neither of which is the persistent-viewport 3D-mode scene §5.1/§11 describe:

- [`createScene(canvas)`](../../src/scene.js) (scene.js:42–203) is the **live viewport scene** — it owns `#three-canvas` (index.html:27), is constructed once in [`main.js`](../../src/main.js) (main.js:30), and hosts `CalendarApp` (main.js:32). Its RAF loop lives in `main.js` (`animate()`, main.js:44–51), externally driven. This is the scene that actually fills the viewport — but it renders the **calendar wall**, not a WordWeaver year ring.
- [`WordWeaverScene`](../../src/wordweaver/WordWeaverScene.js) (1085 lines) is a **self-contained satellite scene**: it creates its **own** `<canvas>` inside a passed container (WordWeaverScene.js:43–46), its own `WebGLRenderer` (64–68), camera (53–56), `OrbitControls` (71–77), lights (79–88), and **auto-starts its own RAF in the constructor** (166). It is instantiated on demand — by the WordWeaver app window ([`apps/wordweaver/index.js`](../../src/apps/wordweaver/index.js):66, `new WordWeaverScene(canvasWrap)`) and by [`WordWeaverEmbed`](../../src/wordweaver/WordWeaverEmbed.js) inside `CalendarApp` — and is **`dispose()`-d on close** (WordWeaverScene.js:751+, `cancelAnimationFrame` + listener teardown). It **does** call `createYearLayout(this.scene, getInitialNotes())` (158) and mount the nested `Timeline3DScene` viewport (144). It is the closest thing to the spec's `WordWeaverScene.js`, but it is a **mounted-on-demand embed, not the always-mounted viewport**.

So Phase 5 is fundamentally a **convergence story**, exactly echoing Phase 4's two-week-renderer situation. Headline deltas vs. §5.1/§5.2:

- **No single canonical scene (architectural):** §11 says `WordWeaverScene` is *always mounted, hidden in 2D*. Reality: the viewport scene is `scene.js` (calendar wall) and `WordWeaverScene` is a disposable embed. 5.1.1 must pick the convergence target before any parameter can be "fixed."
- **Three.js is CDN-loaded via an import map, not npm:** `three` is **absent from `package.json`** (only `@tauri-apps/cli` + `serve`); every module does a bare `import * as THREE from "three"` / `"three/examples/jsm/..."`, resolved by an **import map → unpkg** (`three@0.164.0`, index.html:519–525). 0.164 > the §2.3 r152 minimum ✓, but this is a hard network dependency with no vendoring/SRI — a real concern for `serve:pwa` offline and the Tauri build (5.1.10).
- **No WebGL support detection / fallback anywhere (spec-mandated, fully missing):** §5.2 requires detecting WebGL before init and, if absent, forcing `calendarMode="2d"` + a one-time notice + skipping 3D. **Neither** `createScene` nor `WordWeaverScene` guards `new THREE.WebGLRenderer(...)` — it will throw on unsupported/blocked WebGL with no graceful path (5.1.3).
- **Camera parameters diverge from spec, and one is an outright bug:** §5.2 says FOV 60 / near 0.1 / far 1000. `scene.js` uses 52 / 0.1 / **200** (scene.js:60–65); `WordWeaverScene` uses 42 / 0.1 / **80** (WordWeaverScene.js:53). The far plane of 80 **equals** the §5.3 year-ring radius (80 units) — so the camera literally cannot see the far side of the ring (diameter 160 ≫ far 80). 5.1.4 reconciles params and fixes the far/ring relationship.
- **Renderer `alpha` is `false`, but §5.2 step 1 says alpha enabled — and the fade needs it:** both renderers pass `alpha: false` (scene.js:51, WordWeaverScene.js:67). §3 line 77 requires the 3D canvas to **fade out** during 3D→2D; a transparent (alpha) buffer is what lets the canvas cross-fade cleanly over the DOM grid. 5.1.2 reconciles (tech-stack line 148 only mandates antialias, so this is a genuine spec-vs-code decision).
- **Lighting rigs differ from spec and from each other:** §5.2 = Ambient 0.4 + 1 Directional 0.8 + Hemisphere 0.3. `scene.js` runs a much richer 6-light rig (Hemi 0.55, Ambient 0.35, key Dir 1.05, rim Dir 0.45, fill Point 0.5, warm Point 0.15; scene.js:77–99) plus `FogExp2`; `WordWeaverScene` runs Ambient 1.15 + key Dir 1.25 + rim Point 0.85 and **no hemisphere** (WordWeaverScene.js:79–88), with `scene.fog = null`. 5.1.5 reconciles to a defined rig (the spec values are a floor, not necessarily the ceiling).
- **Two RAF loops / two WebGL contexts can run at once:** `WordWeaverScene` starts its own RAF in the constructor (166) **in addition to** `main.js`'s loop for `scene.js`. When the embed is open, two GL contexts render simultaneously. `WordWeaverScene._tick` *does* already gate the actual `renderer.render(...)` and layout/camera updates on `getCalendarMode() === "3d"` (WordWeaverScene.js:734–746) — good instinct — but it never stops the RAF or releases its context. 5.1.6 reconciles loop ownership.
- **Scene is destroyed, not hidden, on mode/window change:** §5.1/§11 require persist-in-memory + visibility-only toggling with a 400 ms fade. `WordWeaverScene.dispose()` fully tears down (cancel RAF, remove listeners, dispose sub-systems; WordWeaverScene.js:751+) and the embed re-instantiates on reopen. 5.1.8 converges on hide/show.
- **The 3D stack runs on a *second*, duplicate event bus and ignores the §13.2 catalog:** there are **two** bus modules — [`src/utils/EventBus.js`](../../src/utils/EventBus.js) (the Phase-2 bus) and [`src/wordweaver/EventBus.js`](../../src/wordweaver/EventBus.js) (a minimal duplicate: `on/off/emit` over a `Map`). `WordWeaverScene` imports the **wordweaver** one (WordWeaverScene.js:10) and subscribes only to **`timelineUpdated`** (156) — **not** the §13.2 `initialized / eventCreated / eventUpdated / eventDeleted / starterDataCleared`, and it emits **no** `dayFocused/weekFocused/monthFocused`. Mode changes flow through a standalone [`calendarMode.js`](../../src/wordweaver/calendarMode.js) store (`getCalendarMode/setCalendarMode/onCalendarModeChange`), not a §13.2 `modeChanged`/`navigateTo` event; `WordWeaverScene` even reaches over to call `cal2d.hide()/show()` directly (WordWeaverScene.js:191–192). 5.1.9 is the bus convergence — the recurring Phase-4 gap, now a literal duplicate to retire.
- **What's already right (keep):** `pixelRatio = Math.min(devicePixelRatio, 2)` in both (scene.js:53, WordWeaverScene.js:69) matches §2.3/§5.2 exactly; `WordWeaverScene` already reads events only from `timelineModel.js` (§2.3 read-only ✓), already calls `createYearLayout` at init (step 7 ✓), already registers resize + a `ResizeObserver` (WordWeaverScene.js:136–142, more than the spec's bare resize handler), and already mode-gates its render. The bones of §5.2 exist in `WordWeaverScene`; this milestone hardens and de-duplicates them.

---

## 5.1.1 — Designate the canonical `WordWeaverScene` and resolve the two-scene split

- **Purpose:** Decide, once, which Three.js scene *is* the §5.1/§11 WordWeaver viewport scene, so every later parameter and lifecycle task has one owner — eliminating the ambiguity between `scene.js` (viewport, calendar wall) and `WordWeaverScene` (disposable embed).
- **Dependencies:** None (this is the gating decision); informed by main.js:30–32, WordWeaverScene.js:35–166, WordWeaverEmbed.js, apps/wordweaver/index.js:66.
- **Acceptance criteria:** A written decision records (a) which class owns the persistent full-viewport 3D-mode scene, (b) the fate of the other (retire / repurpose to the satellite "single-day weave" window / merge), and (c) the canvas strategy (`#three-canvas` shared vs. a dedicated WordWeaver canvas behind the UI shell); the spec's "scene root = `WordWeaverScene.js`" (§2.3 line 144) is reconciled with the fact that the live viewport root is `scene.js`.
- **Implementation notes:** Recommended target — promote `WordWeaverScene` to the canonical *3D-mode* viewport scene (it already does year layout + timeline + mode-gated render + read-only model access), mounted **once** behind the UI shell and toggled by `calendarMode`, and reclassify `scene.js`'s calendar-wall scene as either the legacy/calendar-wall surface or fold its richer lighting/fog into `WordWeaverScene`. Keep `apps/wordweaver/index.js`'s use of `WordWeaverScene` as a *segment* window distinct from the *year* viewport, or unify under one instance with a layout mode — call this out explicitly so 5.2 knows whether year + segment share an instance.
- **Edge cases:** `CalendarApp` already constructs `scene.js` at boot (main.js:30) and embeds `WordWeaverScene` (WordWeaverEmbed); changing ownership must not leave two viewport canvases stacked; embedded (`?embedded`) mode (main.js:10–13) must still pick exactly one scene.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Only one 3D surface should ever be visible behind the shell; the satellite day-weave window (if kept) is a distinct, clearly-framed sub-experience, not a second viewport.
- **Data flow notes:** Whichever scene is canonical is the §13.2 consumer of `initialized/eventCreated/...` (5.1.9) and the emitter of `*Focused` — the decision here fixes *who* subscribes.
- **Testing notes:** After the decision, exactly one full-viewport WebGL context exists in 3D mode (assert via `renderer.info` / a single canvas behind the shell); the retired/!repurposed path no longer creates a second viewport.
- **Performance notes:** Collapsing to one viewport context is itself the biggest perf win in this milestone (see 5.1.6 — two contexts today).
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Same canonical scene both; mobile just swaps controls (5.5) — not a second scene.
- **Integration points:** §2.3 line 144, §5.1, §11 line 1005, and every subsequent 5.1 task.
- **Status:** **Divergent / undecided** (two scenes, neither matching the spec's role). **Next:** record the convergence target (recommend: `WordWeaverScene` as canonical 3D-mode viewport) before touching parameters.

## 5.1.2 — `WebGLRenderer` creation & parameters (antialias, alpha, pixel-ratio cap)

- **Purpose:** Standardize renderer construction on the canonical scene to the §5.2/§2.3 contract and resolve the `alpha` divergence the 3D↔2D fade depends on.
- **Dependencies:** 5.1.1 (which scene); §5.2 step 1–2, §2.3 line 148.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Canonical renderer is `new THREE.WebGLRenderer({ canvas, antialias: true, alpha: <decided> })` with `setPixelRatio(Math.min(window.devicePixelRatio, 2))`; the `alpha` choice is documented and consistent with the 5.1.8 fade approach; tone-mapping/shadow settings (currently scene.js:55–58 only) are decided for the canonical scene.
- **Implementation notes:** `pixelRatio = Math.min(devicePixelRatio, 2)` already matches in both (scene.js:53, WordWeaverScene.js:69) — keep verbatim. The decision is `alpha`: §5.2 step 1 says *alpha enabled*; both renderers use `alpha:false` (scene.js:51, WordWeaverScene.js:67). If the fade (5.1.8) cross-fades the canvas *opacity* over the DOM grid, `alpha:false` + CSS opacity is acceptable; if the grid must show *through* the canvas mid-fade, `alpha:true` is required. Recommend `alpha:true` to honor the spec and keep the fade option open, accepting the minor fill cost. Carry over `scene.js`'s `ACESFilmicToneMapping` + `PCFSoftShadowMap` only if the WordWeaver look wants them (it currently sets none).
- **Edge cases:** `devicePixelRatio` changing across monitors (re-clamp on resize, 5.1.7); `alpha:true` with a `scene.background` set defeats transparency — if alpha is chosen, background must be `null` (WordWeaverScene already sets `scene.background = null`, WordWeaverScene.js:49) or a CSS backdrop.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Capped pixel ratio protects retina/4K perf; antialias on keeps glass-panel edges (§5.4) clean.
- **Data flow notes:** None (pure renderer config).
- **Testing notes:** `renderer.getPixelRatio() <= 2` on a 3×-DPR device; `renderer` constructs without throwing on supported hardware (unsupported path → 5.1.3); with the chosen `alpha`, the 3D→2D fade shows the grid correctly.
- **Performance notes:** Pixel-ratio cap is the primary fill-rate guard; `alpha:true` adds a small per-pixel cost — acceptable.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Cap matters most on high-DPR phones; same code path.
- **Integration points:** §5.2 (steps 1–2), §2.3 line 148, 5.1.8 (fade), 5.1.7 (resize re-clamp).
- **Status:** **Partial** (pixel-ratio cap ✓; `alpha` diverges from spec). **Next:** decide `alpha` against the fade design and unify renderer construction on the canonical scene.

## 5.1.3 — WebGL support detection + graceful 2D fallback + one-time notice

- **Purpose:** Implement the §5.2 **WebGL Fallback** that is entirely missing today, so a device/browser without WebGL drops to 2D cleanly instead of throwing during `new WebGLRenderer`.
- **Dependencies:** 5.1.1, 5.1.2; `calendarMode.js` (`setCalendarMode`); the §13.2/notification surface used by Alerts (Phase 2).
- **Acceptance criteria:** Before constructing the renderer, WebGL availability is detected; if unavailable, `calendarMode` is forced to `"2d"`, a **one-time** user-facing notice is shown, and **3D init is skipped entirely** (no renderer, no RAF) — the 2D calendar remains fully functional; if available, init proceeds normally.
- **Implementation notes:** Add an `isWebGLAvailable()` probe (create a throwaway `<canvas>`, attempt `getContext("webgl2") || getContext("webgl")`, wrap in try/catch) — Three.js ships `WebGL.isWebGLAvailable()` in `examples/jsm/capabilities/WebGL.js`, importable via the existing `"three/"` import-map entry, so prefer that over a hand-roll. Gate the entire canonical-scene bootstrap behind it. The "one-time" flag persists via the same storage/flags layer Phase 1 established (don't re-notify every load). Forcing `"2d"` must go through `setCalendarMode("2d")` (calendarMode.js:41) so the rest of the app reacts.
- **Edge cases:** WebGL present but context creation later fails/blocked (extension, GPU blocklist) → treat like unavailable at init; context **lost after** successful init is a *runtime* concern handled in 5.1.10; headless/SSR/test (`window`/`document` absent) must no-op, not crash; user manually toggling to 3D after a fallback (block it or re-probe).
- **UI/UX considerations:** Notice is informative, not alarming ("3D view isn't available on this device — showing the 2D calendar"), dismissible, shown once.
- **Data flow notes:** Probe → (fail) `setCalendarMode("2d")` + notify + return; (pass) continue. No timeline reads involved.
- **Testing notes:** With WebGL stubbed unavailable, no renderer/RAF is created, mode is `"2d"`, notice fires exactly once across reloads, 2D calendar works; with WebGL available, 3D inits normally and no notice fires.
- **Performance notes:** Negligible (one throwaway context probe at boot); saves the cost/crash of a doomed renderer.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Especially relevant on low-end/older mobile and locked-down browsers.
- **Integration points:** §5.2 (WebGL Fallback), `calendarMode.js`, Phase-2 notifications, Phase-1 one-time flags.
- **Status:** **Missing** (no detection or fallback in either scene — `new WebGLRenderer` is unguarded). **Next:** add `isWebGLAvailable()` guard around the canonical bootstrap with `setCalendarMode("2d")` + one-time notice.

## 5.1.4 — `PerspectiveCamera` parameters + year-overview placement (fix far-plane vs ring radius)

- **Purpose:** Bring the camera to the §5.2 spec (FOV 60 / near 0.1 / far 1000) and fix the latent bug where the far plane is smaller than the year ring it must show.
- **Dependencies:** 5.1.1; §5.2 steps 3–4; §5.3 ring radius 80 (forward dependency on 5.2).
- **Acceptance criteria:** Canonical camera is `PerspectiveCamera(60, aspect, 0.1, 1000)`; far plane comfortably exceeds the §5.3 ring diameter (≥ ~2× the 80-unit radius plus camera standoff); initial position/target place the camera **outside the ring looking inward** for the year overview (§5.3 line 506).
- **Implementation notes:** Replace `WordWeaverScene`'s `PerspectiveCamera(42, 1, 0.1, 80)` (WordWeaverScene.js:53) — note FOV 42, and **far 80 = ring radius**, so the opposite side of the ring (up to ~160 units away) is clipped. Adopt far 1000 per spec (huge margin, fine for a 80-unit world). FOV: spec says 60; current 42 is tighter/more telephoto (less distortion, narrower view) — if the year ring needs to be framed from outside, 60 likely composes better; document if deviating. Initial `camera.position` should sit outside radius 80 looking at origin; reconcile with `_cameraHome`/`_targetHome` (WordWeaverScene.js:57–58, currently `(0,2.4,8.2)` / `(0,1.1,0)` — a *close* framing, not a ring-overview vantage). The actual fly-in/overview framing is 5.5's job; here just set sane init defaults that can see the whole ring.
- **Edge cases:** `aspect` at construction (use real container aspect, not the hard-coded `1` at WordWeaverScene.js:53 — fixed properly in 5.1.7); extreme aspect ratios (ultrawide / portrait phone) shouldn't clip the ring; near 0.1 is fine but watch z-fighting on thin DayBlock panels (§5.4 `0.15` depth) at far 1000 (depth precision) — log-depth buffer is overkill here but note it.
- **UI/UX considerations:** FOV affects "presence" (§5.1 immersion goal) — too wide distorts, too narrow flattens; 60 is a reasonable immersive default.
- **Data flow notes:** None (camera config); `navigateTo`/focus animations are 5.5.
- **Testing notes:** With the 5.2 ring built, the full ring is visible from the initial camera (no far-plane clipping of the back arc); `camera.far === 1000`, `camera.fov === 60`.
- **Performance notes:** far 1000 over a 80-unit world wastes some depth precision but is harmless; no perf cost.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Aspect differs widely — 5.1.7 keeps `camera.aspect` correct; FOV shared.
- **Integration points:** §5.2 (steps 3–4), §5.3 (ring radius, 5.2), §5.5 (camera framing).
- **Status:** **Divergent + latent bug** (FOV 42 vs 60; **far 80 ≤ ring diameter 160** → back of ring clipped). **Next:** set `(60, aspect, 0.1, 1000)` and an outside-the-ring init vantage.

## 5.1.5 — Lighting rig reconciliation (Ambient + Directional + Hemisphere)

- **Purpose:** Define one lighting rig for the canonical scene that meets §5.2's floor (Ambient 0.4 + Directional 0.8 + Hemisphere 0.3) and supports the §5.4 clean glass-panel look — reconciling the two different existing rigs.
- **Dependencies:** 5.1.1; §5.2 steps 5–6; §5.4 material expectations (`MeshPhysicalMaterial`, transmission).
- **Acceptance criteria:** Canonical scene has at minimum an `AmbientLight`, one `DirectionalLight` (top-right-front), and a `HemisphereLight` (pale-blue sky / dark-grey ground) per §5.2; intensities are documented; the rig produces the "clean glass" look (§5.4) with **no** cracked/fracture/distortion effects (§ line 530, 971).
- **Implementation notes:** `WordWeaverScene` currently has Ambient 1.15 + key Directional 1.25 + rim Point 0.85 and **no hemisphere** (WordWeaverScene.js:79–88); `scene.js` has the fuller cinematic rig + `FogExp2` (scene.js:77–107). The spec values are a *floor*; recommend: keep the directional key, **add the missing `HemisphereLight`** for soft fill per §5.2 step 6, and tune ambient down toward spec so transmissive glass (§5.4 `transmission:0.7`) reads (too much ambient washes out transmission). Preserve `WordWeaverScene`'s layer scheme (ENV_LAYER 0 / CONTENT_LAYER 1, WordWeaverScene.js:26–27, 84–87) — lights must `enable` both layers (as they already do at 84–87) or the GLB environment + content won't both light. Decide fog: spec doesn't mandate it for WordWeaver; `WordWeaverScene` runs `fog=null` (49) while `scene.js` uses `FogExp2` — a tasteful far-fog could help the 80-unit ring read depth, but keep it optional.
- **Edge cases:** Transmissive materials + heavy ambient (low contrast); shadows (scene.js enables `shadowMap`; WordWeaver doesn't) — decide per perf budget (`perfFlags.js`), shadows are expensive on mobile; the GLB environment layer must remain lit independently of content.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Lighting sets the "spatial landscape" mood (§5.1); avoid blown highlights on glass; keep within the no-cracked/clean constraint (§ line 971).
- **Data flow notes:** None.
- **Testing notes:** Scene contains Ambient + Directional + Hemisphere; DayBlock glass panels (5.3) read as clean glass under the rig; no banding/wash-out; shadows (if enabled) gated by perf flags.
- **Performance notes:** Hemisphere + ambient + one directional is cheap; **shadows are the expensive choice** — gate via `isHighPerformance()` (perfFlags.js) as `scene.js` patterns already suggest.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Disable/soften shadows on mobile; same light set.
- **Integration points:** §5.2 (steps 5–6), §5.4, § line 971, `perfFlags.js`.
- **Status:** **Partial / divergent** (has Ambient+Directional but **no Hemisphere**; intensities differ from spec and between the two scenes). **Next:** add `HemisphereLight`, tune intensities toward the spec floor, decide shadows/fog via perf flags.

## 5.1.6 — Single persistent animation loop + loop ownership (no double WebGL context)

- **Purpose:** Establish exactly one RAF render loop for the canonical scene (§5.2 step 8) and eliminate the current two-loops/two-contexts situation, while keeping the loop persistent (§5.1) and mode-aware.
- **Dependencies:** 5.1.1 (canonical scene), 5.1.8 (visibility), 5.1.9 (mode source).
- **Acceptance criteria:** One RAF loop drives the canonical scene; it is started as the last init step (§5.2 step 8); when not in 3D mode it does no GPU work (skips `renderer.render` and per-frame layout/camera updates) but the loop/context **persists** (not torn down); there is never more than one live viewport WebGL context.
- **Implementation notes:** `WordWeaverScene._tick` (WordWeaverScene.js:711–749) already gates `renderer.render` and the heavy `_yearLayout/_timelineViewport/_updateCameraFocus` updates on `getCalendarMode() === "3d"` (734–746) — **keep this pattern**, it's correct. The problems are (a) it auto-starts in the constructor (166) regardless of mode, and (b) `scene.js`'s loop in `main.js` (44–51) runs the *other* context simultaneously. Resolution flows from 5.1.1: if `WordWeaverScene` is canonical, `main.js`'s `animate()` should drive *it* (or the calendar-wall scene is retired), not a second scene. Continue running the RAF even when idle in 2D (cheap — a mode check + early return) so resuming 3D is instant per §5.1 "persists in memory"; the alternative (stop RAF in 2D, restart on 3D) saves the idle check but risks a hitch — document the choice. Source `getCalendarMode()` via the 5.1.9 bus convergence rather than the standalone store if that lands.
- **Edge cases:** Tab hidden (`document.hidden`) — consider pausing RAF to save battery, but ensure resume on `visibilitychange`; mode toggled mid-frame; embedded mode; the satellite day-weave window (if kept, 5.1.1) needs its own loop only while open and disposed on close — that's the *one* legitimate second context, and it must not overlap the year viewport.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Instant 3D resume (persistent loop) supports the seamless 2D↔3D transition (§6.8 / §3 line 77).
- **Data flow notes:** Loop calls `controls.update()` + `renderer.render`; layout/atom updates only in 3D.
- **Testing notes:** In 2D mode, `renderer.info.render.calls` stays flat (no draws) yet the loop is alive; exactly one viewport context across mode toggles; switching to 3D resumes rendering within one frame.
- **Performance notes:** **This is the milestone's key perf fix** — one context instead of two; idle-loop cost is a single comparison per frame.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Battery — consider `document.hidden` pause on mobile especially.
- **Integration points:** §5.2 (step 8), §5.1, §6.8, §3 line 77, `calendarMode.js`/5.1.9.
- **Status:** **Partial** (render is mode-gated ✓, but RAF auto-starts and a *second* `scene.js` loop/context runs in parallel). **Next:** collapse to one canonical loop/context per 5.1.1; keep the mode-gated render; decide idle-loop vs stop/restart.

## 5.1.7 — Resize handling (window + ResizeObserver, aspect, pixel-ratio re-clamp)

- **Purpose:** Keep the renderer size, camera aspect, and pixel ratio correct across window/container/DPR changes (§5.2 step 9), reconciling the two scenes' resize approaches.
- **Dependencies:** 5.1.1, 5.1.2 (pixel-ratio cap), 5.1.4 (camera aspect).
- **Acceptance criteria:** On resize, `renderer.setSize(w, h)` + `camera.aspect = w/h` + `camera.updateProjectionMatrix()` run; pixel ratio is re-clamped to `min(dpr, 2)` (handles monitor moves); both the window `resize` event and a container `ResizeObserver` are handled (the canonical scene is container-sized, not window-sized, if it sits behind the shell with insets); handler is removed on teardown.
- **Implementation notes:** `WordWeaverScene` already does the better job — window `resize` + `ResizeObserver(container)` (WordWeaverScene.js:136–142) calling `_resize()`, removed in `dispose()` (760–762). `scene.js` only does window `resize` with `innerWidth/innerHeight` (scene.js:109–117) — fine for a fullscreen canvas but wrong if the canonical scene is container-insets. Fix the construction-time hard-coded `aspect: 1` (WordWeaverScene.js:53) by computing real aspect in `_resize()` and calling it once at init (it already calls `this._resize()` at 143). Re-apply `setPixelRatio(min(dpr,2))` inside resize so dragging between a 1× and 2× monitor stays crisp/capped.
- **Edge cases:** Zero-size container (collapsed/hidden panel) → guard against `aspect = w/0` (NaN) and `setSize(0,0)`; orientation change on mobile (covered by ResizeObserver/resize); DPR change without size change (monitor swap) — re-clamp pixel ratio; rapid resize storms — the ResizeObserver already coalesces to frames.
- **UI/UX considerations:** No stretch/squish on resize; ring framing (5.1.4) holds across aspect ratios.
- **Data flow notes:** None.
- **Testing notes:** Resize window → no distortion, `camera.aspect` updates; collapse container to 0 → no NaN/crash; move to a different-DPR monitor → pixel ratio re-clamps to ≤2; handlers removed on dispose/hide.
- **Performance notes:** Coalesced via RO/frame; `setSize` reallocates buffers — avoid calling on no-op sizes (guard).
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Orientation changes are the common mobile case; ResizeObserver covers both.
- **Integration points:** §5.2 (step 9), 5.1.2, 5.1.4.
- **Status:** **Good in `WordWeaverScene`, weaker in `scene.js`** (RO + window vs window-only; construction aspect hard-coded `1`). **Next:** standardize on window + container RO + zero-size guard + pixel-ratio re-clamp on the canonical scene.

## 5.1.8 — Scene persistence & mode visibility (hide, don't destroy; 400 ms fade)

- **Purpose:** Make the canonical scene match §5.1/§11 "always mounted, hidden in 2D, not destroyed," with the §3 line 77 400 ms cross-fade — instead of today's dispose-on-close embed.
- **Dependencies:** 5.1.1, 5.1.6 (persistent loop), 5.1.9 (mode source), 5.1.2 (alpha decision for the fade).
- **Acceptance criteria:** Switching to 2D **hides** the 3D canvas (CSS opacity/visibility) and pauses GPU work (5.1.6) but keeps the scene/renderer/objects in memory; switching to 3D shows it; the transition is a 400 ms ease-in-out fade (§3 line 77); the 3D scene is **not** `dispose()`-d on a mode switch — only on app teardown.
- **Implementation notes:** Today `WordWeaverScene` is created per embed mount and `dispose()`-d on close (apps/wordweaver/index.js:122–124; WordWeaverScene.js:751+ tears everything down). Reframe: instantiate the canonical scene once (5.1.1) and toggle a CSS class for the fade rather than mounting/unmounting. It already drives `cal2d.hide()/show()` from mode (WordWeaverScene.js:191–192) — invert/extend that so the **3D canvas** fades opposite the 2D grid. Use CSS `transition: opacity 400ms ease-in-out` on the canvas (and the DOM grid) — this is why 5.1.2's `alpha` choice matters: with `alpha:false`, fade the canvas opacity over the grid (works); with `alpha:true`, the grid can show through mid-fade. Reserve `dispose()` for genuine app teardown / WebGL-context release. Keep the satellite day-weave window's own create/dispose lifecycle separate (it's a transient window, not the persistent viewport).
- **Edge cases:** Rapid 2D↔3D toggling mid-fade (cancel/queue transitions, don't stack); first load directly into 2D (scene still constructed but hidden, per "always mounted" — unless WebGL fell back, 5.1.3); memory pressure on low-end devices (persisting a full scene costs RAM — document, and let the 5.1.3 fallback path keep it unmounted); `prefers-reduced-motion` → skip/shorten the fade.
- **UI/UX considerations:** The 400 ms cross-fade is the signature 2D↔3D transition (§6.8 continuity); no flash of unstyled/blank canvas; reduced-motion respected.
- **Data flow notes:** Mode change (5.1.9) → toggle visibility classes + 5.1.6 render gate; focused-date continuity across the transition is §6.8 (the scene keeps its state because it's not destroyed).
- **Testing notes:** Toggle to 2D → canvas hidden, scene object still in memory (same instance reference), `renderer` not disposed; toggle back → same instance, fades in over ~400 ms; reduced-motion → instant; rapid toggles don't leak transitions.
- **Performance notes:** Persisting costs RAM but saves re-init cost and enables instant transitions; pair with 5.1.6 idle-gating so persistence isn't also burning GPU.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** On very low-RAM mobile, persistence is the riskiest spec requirement — note a possible mobile exception (dispose in 2D) if profiling demands it.
- **Integration points:** §5.1, §11 line 1005, §3 line 77, §6.8, 5.1.2/5.1.6/5.1.9.
- **Status:** **Contradicts spec** (embed is created on mount and `dispose()`-d on close; no fade). **Next:** instantiate once, toggle visibility with a 400 ms fade, reserve `dispose()` for app teardown.

## 5.1.9 — Event-bus integration at init + retire the duplicate `wordweaver/EventBus.js`

- **Purpose:** Wire the canonical scene to the §13.2 catalog on the **Phase-2 bus** and converge the two event-bus implementations + the standalone mode store — the recurring Phase-4 bus gap, here a literal duplicate.
- **Dependencies:** 5.1.1; Phase-2 `src/utils/EventBus.js`; `timelineModel.js`; `calendarMode.js`; Phase-4's identical `Calendar2D` bus migration.
- **Acceptance criteria:** At init the canonical scene subscribes (on the Phase-2 bus) to §13.2 `initialized {eventCount}`, `eventCreated`, `eventUpdated`, `eventDeleted`, `starterDataCleared`, and `navigateTo` (line 436); it emits `dayFocused/weekFocused/monthFocused` (§13.2 lines 1167–1169) on the appropriate focus actions; mode changes are observed via a §13.2 `modeChanged` (or the agreed shared seam) rather than only the standalone store; the duplicate `src/wordweaver/EventBus.js` is either retired in favor of `src/utils/EventBus.js` or explicitly justified, with a single source of truth documented.
- **Implementation notes:** Today `WordWeaverScene` imports `on/off` from `src/wordweaver/EventBus.js` (WordWeaverScene.js:10) — a separate `Map`-backed `on/off/emit` — and listens only to **`timelineUpdated`** (156), rebuilding the year layout on it. That single signal must be mapped to the §13.2 set so the 3D scene reacts to create/update/delete/clear like §13.2 line 1161–1164 specifies, and emits focus events for Inkling (lines 1167–1169). Mode currently flows through `calendarMode.js` (`onCalendarModeChange`, WordWeaverScene.js:21) with direct `cal2d.hide()/show()` calls (191–192) — converge this onto a §13.2 `modeChanged` event (the same gap Phase 3's `preferenceChanged` flagged; note §13.2 doesn't yet list `modeChanged`/`preferenceChanged` — flag for a spec-catalog update, consistent with the Phase-3 carry-forward). Decide the duplicate bus: ideally retire `wordweaver/EventBus.js` and route everything through `utils/EventBus.js` (one bus app-wide); if the wordweaver bus must stay for internal high-frequency signals, document the boundary. This pairs with Phase-4's `Calendar2D` migration so both 2D and 3D finally listen to the **same** `navigateTo` (line 436 explicitly names both as listeners).
- **Edge cases:** `initialized` may fire before the scene subscribes (boot ordering) — subscribe before timeline init or replay last `initialized`; double-subscription on re-show (must not re-subscribe if persistent per 5.1.8 — subscribe once at construct, not per show); event payload shape drift between the two buses; unsubscribing on real teardown only.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Correct subscriptions are what make the 3D scene update live when events change in Inkling/2D (the §12 read/write paths, lines 1089/1101) — invisible when right, broken-feeling when missing.
- **Data flow notes:** `timelineModel` emits §13.2 events → canonical scene re-renders affected DayBlocks (batched to next frame, § line 552, detailed in 5.3) → scene emits `*Focused` → Inkling reacts; `navigateTo` → scene focuses (5.5). Reads stay model-only (§2.3).
- **Testing notes:** Firing `eventCreated/Updated/Deleted/starterDataCleared` on the Phase-2 bus updates the 3D scene; the scene emits `dayFocused` on a day focus; `navigateTo {level,...}` is handled by both `WordWeaverScene` and `Calendar2D`; only one bus module remains (or the split is documented); no duplicate subscriptions after a hide/show.
- **Performance notes:** Re-renders batched to the next animation frame (§ line 552) — not per-event; subscriptions are O(1).
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Identical.
- **Integration points:** §13.2 (lines 1160–1169), line 436, §12 (lines 1089/1101), §2.3, `utils/EventBus.js`, `calendarMode.js`, Phase-4 `Calendar2D` migration, Phase-3 `preferenceChanged`/`modeChanged` catalog gap.
- **Status:** **Divergent** (runs on a **duplicate** `wordweaver/EventBus.js`, listens only to `timelineUpdated`, emits no `*Focused`, mode via a standalone store + direct `cal2d` calls). **Next:** subscribe to the §13.2 set on the Phase-2 bus, emit focus events, converge/retire the duplicate bus, route mode via `modeChanged`.

## 5.1.10 — Three.js delivery (CDN import map) reconciliation + WebGL context-loss/restore + disposal completeness

- **Purpose:** Make the engine provisioning and teardown robust at the init layer — pin/justify the CDN import map for PWA/Tauri, handle WebGL context loss/restore, and confirm full GPU resource disposal — so init has a clean inverse.
- **Dependencies:** 5.1.1, 5.1.2 (renderer), 5.1.3 (init-time WebGL guard), `scripts/build-web.mjs`, `index.html` import map, Tauri config.
- **Acceptance criteria:** The Three.js delivery is documented and decided (CDN import map vs. vendored/bundled) with the §2.3 r152-minimum satisfied (currently `three@0.164.0` ✓) and the offline/PWA/Tauri implications resolved; the canonical renderer registers `webglcontextlost`/`webglcontextrestored` handlers (prevent default on loss, rebuild on restore); `dispose()` releases the renderer context and disposes geometries/materials/textures (no GPU leak), and is the documented inverse of init.
- **Implementation notes:** **Delivery:** all modules use bare `"three"` / `"three/examples/jsm/..."` resolved by the import map → unpkg (`three@0.164.0`, index.html:519–525); `three` is **not** in `package.json`. This works in a live browser but: (a) `npm run serve:pwa` offline will fail without network/SW caching of unpkg; (b) the Tauri build (`tauri:build`) ships a webview that also hits unpkg at runtime — fragile/offline-hostile; (c) no SRI/integrity pinning beyond the version. Options: keep CDN but **cache the three modules in the service worker / `public`**, or **vendor** `three.module.js` + the used `examples/jsm` addons locally and point the import map at a relative path (then `build-web.mjs` copies them). Recommend vendoring for PWA/Tauri robustness; at minimum document the dependency. **Context loss:** neither scene handles it — add `canvas.addEventListener("webglcontextlost", e => e.preventDefault())` + `"webglcontextrestored"` to rebuild renderer state; pairs with the 5.1.3 *init-time* WebGL guard (that's pre-init absence; this is post-init loss). **Disposal:** `WordWeaverScene.dispose()` (WordWeaverScene.js:751+) cancels RAF, removes listeners, disposes sub-systems and the GLB env — verify it also calls `renderer.dispose()` / `renderer.forceContextLoss()` and disposes geometries/materials/textures created here, so a real teardown (app exit) leaks nothing; keep dispose as the documented inverse even though mode-switches now hide (5.1.8) rather than dispose.
- **Edge cases:** unpkg outage/slow network at boot (consider a local fallback or a clear error); addons pulled from `examples/jsm` must match the core version (the `"three/"` map entry already pins both to `0.164.0` ✓); context loss during the 2D-hidden state (rebuild lazily on next show); double-dispose; disposing while a fade (5.1.8) is mid-flight.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Context-loss recovery prevents the dreaded permanent black canvas after a GPU reset/driver hiccup; offline PWA users shouldn't get a blank 3D mode.
- **Data flow notes:** None at the model layer; this is engine/resource lifecycle.
- **Testing notes:** Force a context loss (WEBGL_lose_context extension) → no crash, scene restores on restore; `package.json`/import-map decision recorded; after `dispose()`, `renderer.info.memory` shows geometries/textures at 0 and the context is released; offline load behaves per the chosen delivery strategy.
- **Performance notes:** Proper disposal prevents GPU memory leaks across the satellite-window open/close cycle; context-loss handling avoids a doomed running loop on a dead context.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Mobile GPUs lose context more often (backgrounding, memory pressure) — context-loss handling matters most there; offline/PWA is a mobile-common case.
- **Integration points:** §2.3 (r152 / line 143), import map (index.html:519–525), `scripts/build-web.mjs`, Tauri build, 5.1.2/5.1.3, WordWeaverScene.js:751+ disposal.
- **Status:** **Gap** (CDN import map works live but is offline/Tauri-fragile and unvendored; **no context-loss handling**; disposal completeness for renderer/GPU resources unverified). **Next:** decide vendor-vs-CDN, add `webglcontextlost/restored` handlers, and confirm `renderer.dispose()` + resource disposal close the loop.


---

<!-- source: phase-05-milestone-5.2-year-layout.md -->

# Phase 5 — Milestone 5.2: Year Layout (Expanded)

**Goal:** Specify the year-overview construction (`createYearLayout()` — §5.2 step 7, detailed in §5.3): 12 month clusters placed in a ring around the origin, each rotated `(monthIndex / 12) · 2π`, each holding a 7-column `DayBlock3D` grid + a centered month label + a few perimeter `AtomGlyph3D` decorations, with the spec's instance-optimization (`InstancedMesh` for like-materialed day blocks, shared atom geometry, pre-rasterized label textures). This milestone is grounded against the **already-existing** year layout — `WordWeaverYearLayout3D` and its helpers (`createYearLayout` / `populateMonthCluster` / `createMonthLabelMesh`) live inside [`WordWeaverScene.js`](../../src/wordweaver/WordWeaverScene.js):780–1085 — so every task reconciles built / gap / next-step rather than describing greenfield. It does **not** cover the per-day glass panel internals (§5.4 → 5.3) or atom internals (§5.5 → 5.4); those are downstream. It covers how the year is *assembled and arranged*.

**Spec alignment:** §5.3 (Year Layout: ring of 12 month clusters, **radius 80**, rotation `(monthIndex/12)·2π`, clusters face inward / camera outside looking in (line 506); per-cluster: month label centered above, **7×4-or-5** `DayBlock3D` grid, leading/trailing empty cells as **invisible/dimmed blocks** for alignment (line 511), 2–4 perimeter `AtomGlyph3D`; **instance optimization** — like-material day blocks merged into `InstancedMesh`, atoms share one `SphereGeometry`/material, labels use **pre-rasterized canvas textures** not per-frame `TextGeometry`, lines 514–517), §5.2 step 7 (`createYearLayout()` is the 7th init step), §5.4 line 552 (on `eventCreated/Updated/Deleted` the scene identifies **affected** `DayBlock3D`s and queues a canvas re-render **batched to next frame** — i.e. *targeted*, not full rebuild), §16.2 line 1317 (LOD: at year-overview distance, `DayBlock3D` canvas textures are **low-res 128×160**), §16.5 line 1479-1480 (instanced rendering of same-dimension day blocks + automatic frustum culling of off-screen clusters), §2.3 line 157 (both renderers read events **exclusively** through `timelineModel.js`; on mutation each re-renders its **visible portion** via the bus), §13.2 line 1169/1171 (`monthFocused {year, month}` emitted by `WordWeaverScene`; `navigateTo {level}` consumed by it — the focus hooks the year layout must expose).

**Sequencing:** Second milestone of Phase 5; it is literally step 7 of the §5.2 sequence 5.1 just finished plumbing. It depends on 5.1's canonical-scene decision (5.1.1) — the year layout attaches to *that* scene's root — and on 5.1.4's camera params (the ring must be visible from the year-overview vantage; note the **radius/far-plane interaction** 5.1.4 already flagged). It feeds 5.3 (DayBlock3D internals — the grid cells this milestone positions), 5.4 (AtomGlyph3D internals — the decorations this milestone places), 5.5 (camera focus on a month cluster / day block — this milestone exposes the `monthClusters` / `findDayBlock` handles 5.5 animates to), and 5.6 (interaction — raycasting the day blocks this milestone lays out). Carries Phase 5's recurring finding: the layout rebuilds on the **duplicate** `wordweaver/EventBus.js` `timelineUpdated` signal, not the §13.2 catalog (5.1.9), and it does a **full teardown+rebuild** rather than the §5.4 targeted re-render — the central efficiency reconciliation here.

---

## Current implementation status (reconciliation)

The year layout **already exists and is substantially built** — this is an upgrade/reconcile milestone, not an introduction. [`WordWeaverYearLayout3D`](../../src/wordweaver/WordWeaverScene.js):945–1065 (constructed by `createYearLayout`, WordWeaverScene.js:1073) builds a `THREE.Group` root (`ww-year-layout-3d`) holding 12 month-cluster `Group`s, each populated by `populateMonthCluster` (855–940) with week halos, background atoms, a month label, and one `DayBlock3D` per real day. It tracks `dayBlocks` / `bgAtoms` / `weekHalos` / `monthClusters` / `focusedDay`, exposes `findDayBlock(monthIndex, dayIndex)` (1007) and an `update(delta, elapsed, camera)` that bobs labels and sets per-block proximity by camera distance (1018–1035), and disposes via `_clear()` (1037). The bones of §5.3 are present. Headline deltas vs §5.3/§16:

- **Ring radius is 26, spec says 80 (`YEAR_RING_RADIUS = 26`, WordWeaverScene.js:783).** At radius 26 the 12 clusters sit on a circumference of ~163 units → ~13.6 units of arc each, but a cluster's day grid is ~10.5 wide (7 cols × `COL_SPACING` 1.5) and up to ~12 tall (6 rows × `ROW_SPACING` 2) — so clusters nearly collide. Radius 80 (circumference ~503, ~42/cluster) gives the breathing room the spec intends. 5.2.1 reconciles, and this couples to 5.1.4 (camera far plane must exceed the ring; far was the latent bug there).
- **Rotation matches spec ✓ (with a start offset):** `angle = (monthIndex/12)·2π − π/2` (WordWeaverScene.js:978) is exactly the §5.3 `(monthIndex/12)·2π` plus a `−π/2` to start January at the "top/front." Keep — just document the offset.
- **Clusters `lookAt(0,0,0)` — they *face inward* (✓ line 506 clause 1) but the camera vantage contradicts clause 2.** `cluster.lookAt(0,0,0)` (WordWeaverScene.js:984) orients each cluster's front toward the origin. §5.3 line 506 says clusters face inward **and** the camera starts **outside** the ring looking in — but a camera outside a ring of inward-facing clusters sees their **backs**. With the current near-center camera (`_cameraHome (0,2.4,8.2)` inside radius 26), you stand *inside* the ring reading the fronts — a coherent "surrounded by months" vantage, but not the spec's outside-in. 5.2.2 must resolve the orientation-vs-vantage contradiction (either flip clusters to face outward for an outside camera, or formally adopt the inside-the-ring overview and update the spec/5.1.4 vantage).
- **Day grid: 7 columns ✓, but rows run to 6 and week-start is hardcoded Monday.** `cellIndex = monOffset + day − 1`, `col = cellIndex % 7`, `row = ⌊cellIndex/7⌋`, `x = (col−3)·COL_SPACING`, `y = −row·ROW_SPACING` (WordWeaverScene.js:917–921) is a correct calendar grid. But `monOffset` is computed Monday-first and **hardcoded** (`first.getDay() === 0 ? 6 : first.getDay() − 1`, 858) — no week-start preference (the same gap Phase 4.1.3/4.2.2/4.3 carried; closes via the Phase-3 `preferenceChanged` seam). And `weekCount` can be **6** (`Math.ceil((monOffset + daysInMonth)/7)`, 860), while §5.3 line 510 says "4 or 5 rows" — the spec undercounts; reconcile to "4–6." (5.2.3.)
- **No leading/trailing empty cells (spec line 511 wants invisible/dimmed placeholder blocks).** The loop runs `day = 1..daysInMonth` only (WordWeaverScene.js:902); leading/trailing slots are simply absent. Grid *position* stays aligned (because `cellIndex` includes `monOffset`), but there are no dimmed out-of-month placeholders — the same "dimmed out-of-month days" gap Phase 4.2 flagged for the 2D month grid, now in 3D. 5.2.4.
- **Month label is already a pre-rasterized `CanvasTexture` ✓ (good instinct), but per-month and via `MeshStandardMaterial`.** `createMonthLabelMesh` (WordWeaverScene.js:790–820) draws month name + year onto a 512×128 canvas → `CanvasTexture` on a `PlaneGeometry(3.2, 0.8)` — exactly the §5.3 line 517 "pre-rasterized canvas textures to avoid per-frame `TextGeometry`" intent (the spec even offers `TextGeometry` as an alternative at line 509; the code chose the cheaper path — keep). Gaps: a fresh canvas+texture **per cluster** (12 textures, no atlas/cache), it is **not billboarded** (fixed plane; the `update()` only bobs `position.y`, WordWeaverScene.js:1022–1023, it doesn't face camera), and label disposal isn't in `_clear()` (see 5.2.10). 5.2.5.
- **Atoms: count ✓ (2–4), placement & determinism ✗.** `atomCount = 2 + ⌊random·3⌋` = 2–4 per cluster (WordWeaverScene.js:880) matches §5.3 line 512 / §5.5 line 561. But placement is `Math.random()` scattered inside the cluster box (886–892), not the spec's "perimeter / corners or edges of the day grid," and the randomness makes the layout **non-deterministic across rebuilds** (every `timelineUpdated` reshuffles atoms — see 5.2.8). They already use `AtomGlyph3D.createBackground` (shared-geometry path lives in 5.4). 5.2.6.
- **No `InstancedMesh` — the headline perf gap.** §5.3 line 514-515 + §16.5 line 1479 mandate merging same-material `DayBlock3D`s into `InstancedMesh` groups. Reality: each day is an **individual** `DayBlock3D` (WordWeaverScene.js:924–936) → ~365 day-block object graphs + ~24–48 atoms per full year, all separate draw paths. The code *does* pass `useSharedMaterials: true` (930) — a partial nod (shared materials, the §16.5 "pre-created and shared" half) — but geometry/draw-call instancing is absent. 5.2.7.
- **Full rebuild on every mutation, not §5.4 targeted re-render.** `_rebuildYearLayout()` calls `_yearLayout.build(getInitialNotes())` (WordWeaverScene.js:199), and `build()` calls `_clear()` then reconstructs **all 12 clusters / all 365 blocks** (969–1000). This fires on the duplicate-bus `timelineUpdated` (156) — so a single event edit tears down and rebuilds the entire year (and reshuffles random atoms). §5.4 line 552 wants the opposite: identify the **affected** `DayBlock3D`(s) by date and queue **just their** canvas re-render, batched to next frame. This is the milestone's key efficiency reconciliation and dovetails with 5.1.9's bus convergence. 5.2.8.
- **Reads are correctly model-only ✓.** `getEventsForDay(year, monthIndex, day)` and `getInitialNotes()` both come from `timelineModel.js` (WordWeaverScene.js:12–16); the layout holds no event copy — satisfies §2.3 line 157. Keep. (The `Math.random` atoms and `classifyEvent`/`enrichDayEvents` derivation are presentation, not a second source of truth.)
- **Year is hardcoded to the current year; no multi-year navigation.** `const year = new Date().getFullYear()` (WordWeaverScene.js:971); the ring is always *this* calendar year. §5.3 describes "the entire year" without multi-year nav, so this is spec-conformant *today*, but flag it: `navigateTo`/focus across a year boundary has no path. 5.2.10.
- **Week halos are a non-spec embellishment.** Per-week translucent colored planes (`WEEK_HALO_HEX`, WordWeaverScene.js:782, 862–878) are not in §5.3. They're a reasonable depth cue — decide keep-as-enhancement vs trim; either way they must be disposed (they are, in `_clear()` 1049–1053) and counted in the instance budget. 5.2.10.
- **What's already right (keep):** rotation formula ✓, 7-col grid math ✓, month label pre-rasterized ✓, `useSharedMaterials` ✓, atom count 2–4 ✓, model-only reads ✓, `findDayBlock` focus handle ✓, halos/atoms/blocks all disposed in `_clear()` ✓, proximity-based per-block emphasis in `update()` ✓.

---

## 5.2.1 — Ring geometry: radius, 12-cluster placement, rotation

- **Purpose:** Reconcile the ring's geometry to §5.3 — radius **80** (not the current 26), 12 clusters evenly placed, each rotated `(monthIndex/12)·2π` — so clusters don't collide and the year reads as a clean ring.
- **Dependencies:** 5.1.1 (canonical scene the root attaches to), 5.1.4 (camera far plane / vantage — radius and far are coupled); §5.3 lines 503–505.
- **Acceptance criteria:** 12 month clusters are positioned on a ring of radius **80** world units about the origin; cluster *N* sits at angle `(N/12)·2π` (+ the documented `−π/2` start offset); adjacent clusters do not visually overlap given the ~10.5×~12 grid footprint; the value is a named constant (today `YEAR_RING_RADIUS`) and the camera (5.1.4) can frame the whole ring without far-plane clipping.
- **Implementation notes:** Change `YEAR_RING_RADIUS = 26` → `80` (WordWeaverScene.js:783). The placement (`cos(angle)·R`, `0`, `sin(angle)·R`, WordWeaverScene.js:979–983) and rotation (`angle = (monthIndex/12)·2π − π/2`, 978) are already spec-correct — keep, and document the `−π/2` (starts January at −Z "front"). Verify the coupling 5.1.4 flagged: with R=80 the back arc is ~160 units from a front-side camera, so `camera.far` must be ≥ ~200 (5.1.4 sets 1000 ✓). Consider whether cluster footprint should scale with radius or stay fixed (fixed is fine at R=80; at R=26 they were cramped — the radius bump *is* the fix).
- **Edge cases:** Very large day grids (6-row months, 5.2.3) widen the footprint — confirm no overlap at R=80; ultrawide/portrait aspect (5.1.7) shouldn't make near clusters clip the frustum sides; `−π/2` offset must stay consistent with any month-focus math (5.5 / 5.2.9).
- **UI/UX considerations:** The ring is the signature "year as a place" view (§5.1); even spacing + no overlap is what makes it legible.
- **Data flow notes:** None (pure geometry); cluster contents come from 5.2.3/5.2.8.
- **Testing notes:** All 12 cluster `position` vectors lie on radius 80 (`length() ≈ 80`); angular spacing is uniform (`2π/12`); no two cluster bounding boxes intersect; from the 5.1.4 init camera the full ring is within the frustum (no far clip of the back arc).
- **Performance notes:** Radius alone is free; the cost is the *contents* (5.2.7) — a bigger ring doesn't add draw calls.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Same geometry; mobile may need a wider FOV or pull-back to frame R=80 (5.5) — note for the camera milestone.
- **Integration points:** §5.3 (503–505), 5.1.1, 5.1.4, 5.2.2 (orientation), 5.2.9 (month focus uses cluster positions).
- **Status:** **Partial / divergent** (rotation ✓; **radius 26 vs spec 80** → clusters cramped). **Next:** set `YEAR_RING_RADIUS = 80`, keep the rotation+offset, re-verify against the 5.1.4 camera far plane.

## 5.2.2 — Cluster orientation vs camera vantage (face-inward / outside-looking-in contradiction)

- **Purpose:** Resolve the §5.3 line 506 tension — clusters "face inward (toward origin)" **and** "camera starts outside the ring looking in" are mutually exclusive as written — and make the chosen vantage consistent with the actual `cluster.lookAt(0,0,0)` and the 5.1.4 camera home.
- **Dependencies:** 5.2.1 (radius), 5.1.4 (camera init), 5.5 (camera framing/focus); §5.3 line 506.
- **Acceptance criteria:** A written decision states whether (a) clusters face **outward** and the camera orbits **outside** the ring looking in (spec clause 2), or (b) clusters face **inward** and the camera sits **inside/near-center** looking out at the surrounding months (matches current `lookAt(0,0,0)` + near-center `_cameraHome`); the month labels and day-grid text are oriented to be **readable** from whichever vantage is chosen; 5.1.4's "outside-the-ring init vantage" recommendation is reconciled with this decision (one of them changes).
- **Implementation notes:** Current `cluster.lookAt(0,0,0)` (WordWeaverScene.js:984) = fronts toward origin → readable from inside. `_cameraHome (0,2.4,8.2)` (5.1.4 context) is inside radius 26 → today you read fronts from within the ring (vantage **b**). But 5.1.4's acceptance text adopted "outside the ring looking inward" (spec clause 2) → that would show cluster **backs** unless clusters face outward (drop the `lookAt`, or `lookAt` a point *beyond* origin). Pick one and make 5.1.4 + this task agree. Recommendation: vantage **(b) inside-the-ring** is the more striking "immersed in your year" view and matches the built code with the least churn — if chosen, update 5.1.4's init vantage note from "outside looking in" to "near-center looking out," and keep `lookAt(0,0,0)`. If clause 2 (outside) is preferred for the overview, flip to `cluster.lookAt(2·position)` (face away from origin) and move the camera outside R=80.
- **Edge cases:** Tilted/elevated camera (the `+2.4` Y) means clusters may need a slight X-tilt to face the camera, not the horizon; month-focus (5.2.9/5.5) must place the camera on the **readable** side; mobile may invert orbit limits.
- **UI/UX considerations:** This decides the whole "what does the year *feel* like" — standing in the middle of your months vs looking at a distant ring; legibility of labels/day text is the hard constraint.
- **Data flow notes:** None.
- **Testing notes:** From the chosen camera home, all 12 month labels and the front of each day grid are **front-facing** (dot(cameraDir, clusterFront) shows fronts, not backs); switching to month-focus keeps the cluster front toward the camera.
- **Performance notes:** None.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Same orientation logic; orbit constraints (5.5) differ.
- **Integration points:** §5.3 line 506, 5.1.4, 5.2.1, 5.5, 5.2.9.
- **Status:** **Spec-internal contradiction + code/spec divergence** (code faces inward + camera near-center; spec line 506 says inward **and** outside-looking-in). **Next:** pick a single vantage (recommend inside-the-ring to match built code) and align 5.1.4 to it.

## 5.2.3 — Day grid construction (7 columns, week rows, week-start preference)

- **Purpose:** Reconcile the per-cluster day grid to §5.3 — 7 columns × the right number of week rows, correctly offset for the first weekday — and close the **hardcoded Monday-start** gap with the Phase-3 `preferenceChanged` seam.
- **Dependencies:** 5.2.1 (cluster frame), 5.2.4 (empty cells share this grid), Phase-3 `preferenceChanged`/Settings week-start (the Phase-4 carry-forward); §5.3 line 510.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Each cluster lays out day blocks in a 7-column grid where column = weekday and row = week-of-month, with the first day offset by the first weekday; the week-start (Mon vs Sun) follows the **user preference** rather than a hardcoded value; the row count is documented as **4–6** (not the spec's "4 or 5" — a spec correction); `COL_SPACING`/`ROW_SPACING` produce a grid that fits the cluster footprint assumed in 5.2.1.
- **Implementation notes:** Keep the grid math (`cellIndex = monOffset + day − 1`, `col = cellIndex%7`, `row = ⌊cellIndex/7⌋`, `x = (col−3)·COL_SPACING`, `y = −row·ROW_SPACING`, WordWeaverScene.js:917–921) — it's correct. Two fixes: (1) replace the hardcoded Monday offset (`first.getDay() === 0 ? 6 : first.getDay() − 1`, 858) with a `weekStart`-aware offset read from the same preference store Phase 4.2.2 used, and re-subscribe to recompute on `preferenceChanged` (note §13.2 still lacks `preferenceChanged` — the Phase-3 carry-forward); (2) document `weekCount = Math.ceil((monOffset + daysInMonth)/7)` (860) can be 6 (e.g. a 31-day month starting on the last grid column) and update the spec's "4 or 5 rows" to "4–6." The `(col − 3)` centering assumes 7 columns centered on column 3 — keep, and if week-start changes the column mapping stays 0–6 so centering is unaffected.
- **Edge cases:** February in a non-leap year starting on the week-start day = exactly 4 rows; a 31-day month offset to the far column = 6 rows; week-start toggled at runtime must rebuild/re-offset (today a full rebuild, 5.2.8 — eventually targeted); DST/timezone in `new Date(year, month-1, 1)` (local-time construction is fine for a calendar but note it).
- **UI/UX considerations:** Grid must read as a familiar month calendar (weekday columns aligned across all 12 clusters); a mismatched week-start vs the 2D views (Calendar2D) would be jarring — they must share the preference.
- **Data flow notes:** Grid structure derives from `year/month` only; event content per cell comes from 5.2.8's `getEventsForDay`.
- **Testing notes:** A month known to span 6 weeks renders 6 rows; switching week-start Mon→Sun shifts the first-day column correctly across all clusters and matches Calendar2D; column count is always 7.
- **Performance notes:** Grid math is trivial; cost is the blocks (5.2.7).
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Identical layout; same preference source.
- **Integration points:** §5.3 line 510, Phase-3 `preferenceChanged`, Phase-4.2.2 week-start, 5.2.4, 5.2.8.
- **Status:** **Partial** (grid math ✓; **week-start hardcoded Monday**, no preference; spec row-count undercount). **Next:** read week-start from the preference seam, re-offset on `preferenceChanged`, correct spec to 4–6 rows.

## 5.2.4 — Leading/trailing empty cells (invisible/dimmed placeholder blocks)

- **Purpose:** Implement §5.3 line 511 — empty cells before the 1st and after the last day are rendered as **invisible/dimmed** blocks to maintain grid alignment — which is entirely absent today.
- **Dependencies:** 5.2.3 (grid), 5.3 (DayBlock3D dimmed/empty state); §5.3 line 511; echoes Phase-4.2 "dimmed out-of-month days."
- **Acceptance criteria:** Each cluster's 7×N grid is fully populated for every cell index `0..(weekCount·7 − 1)`: real days as normal `DayBlock3D`s, and leading (`< monOffset`) / trailing (`≥ monOffset + daysInMonth`) cells as **dimmed or invisible** placeholder blocks occupying the grid slot; alignment across clusters is visually consistent; placeholders are non-interactive (no raycast hit in 5.6).
- **Implementation notes:** Today the loop is `for (day = 1..daysInMonth)` (WordWeaverScene.js:902) — leading/trailing slots are skipped, so the grid is positionally aligned but visually ragged at the corners. Decide the placeholder treatment: (a) **dimmed** out-of-month blocks (matches Phase-4.2's 2D choice and gives a fuller grid — recommended for visual symmetry between 2D/3D), or (b) **invisible** spacers (cheaper, just preserves alignment). Either way, generate them by iterating the full `weekCount·7` cell range and branching real-vs-placeholder on the cell index. If dimmed, render a low-emissive empty `DayBlock3D` variant (5.3 owns that state) and **exclude it from the pickables** (5.6) and from focus/proximity emphasis (`update()` 1027). Keep placeholders out of the §5.4 re-render path (no events to draw).
- **Edge cases:** A month that fills its rows exactly has zero trailing placeholders; placeholders must not be counted as "today" or focusable; if invisible, they still must not block raycasts (set `raycast = () => {}` or omit from pickables); instancing (5.2.7) — placeholders share the dimmed material bucket.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Dimmed placeholders make every cluster a tidy rectangle; invisible ones leave stair-stepped corners — recommend dimmed for polish, consistent with the 2D month grid.
- **Data flow notes:** Placeholders carry no event data — pure layout.
- **Testing notes:** Every cluster grid is a complete `7 × weekCount` rectangle; placeholder cells are visually distinct (dimmed) or absent (invisible) per the decision; placeholders are never returned by the day-picker (5.6) and never focused.
- **Performance notes:** Adds up to ~16 placeholder slots/cluster (~190/year worst case) — fold into the dimmed `InstancedMesh` bucket (5.2.7) so they cost ~one draw, not 190.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Same.
- **Integration points:** §5.3 line 511, 5.2.3, 5.2.7 (instancing bucket), 5.3 (dimmed state), 5.6 (non-pickable).
- **Status:** **Missing** (no leading/trailing cells rendered; corners ragged). **Next:** iterate the full cell range, render out-of-month slots as dimmed (recommended) non-interactive placeholders, fold into the shared instance bucket.

## 5.2.5 — Month label (pre-rasterized texture, placement, billboard, lifecycle)

- **Purpose:** Reconcile the month label to §5.3 lines 509/517 — centered above the cluster, **pre-rasterized** (not per-frame `TextGeometry`), readable from the chosen vantage — and fix its texture caching and disposal.
- **Dependencies:** 5.2.2 (vantage → readability/billboard), 5.2.1 (cluster frame); §5.3 lines 509, 517.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Each cluster shows a month label (month name + year) centered above its grid; the label is rendered once to a **`CanvasTexture`** (no per-frame text regeneration — spec line 517 ✓); the label is **readable** from the 5.2.2 vantage (billboard-to-camera or correctly oriented); label textures/materials are created once and **disposed** in `_clear()`/`dispose()`; the per-month texture creation is either cached/atlased or justified.
- **Implementation notes:** `createMonthLabelMesh` (WordWeaverScene.js:790–820) already does the right thing — 512×128 canvas → `CanvasTexture` on `PlaneGeometry(3.2, 0.8)`, `MeshStandardMaterial` emissive, `renderOrder = 10`, `MONTH_LABEL_Y = 5.2` above the grid. Keep the pre-rasterized approach (cheaper than the spec's alternative `TextGeometry`, line 509). Fixes: (1) the label is a fixed plane — `update()` only bobs `position.y` (1022–1023) — so if 5.2.2 picks an orbiting camera, **billboard** it (face the camera each frame, cheap) or it'll be edge-on from the sides; (2) it builds a **fresh canvas+texture per cluster** (12×) — fine for 12, but consider a shared font/atlas if labels ever animate; (3) **disposal**: `_clear()` (1037–1059) disposes blocks/atoms/halos but **not** the label texture/material (it removes cluster children from `root` but never `tex.dispose()`/`mat.dispose()`) — add label disposal to avoid a GPU texture leak across rebuilds (acute given the full-rebuild-on-mutation in 5.2.8). The label already carries `userData.isMonthLabel` and the year string — keep both.
- **Edge cases:** Long localized month names (`toLocaleDateString` 'long') overflowing the 512px canvas — measure/scale text; high-DPR crispness (the canvas is fixed 512×128 regardless of DPR — consider 2× for retina like §5.4 line 544 does for day blocks); billboard vs the bob animation interacting; year-boundary (5.2.10) would need the year text updated.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Labels are the primary wayfinding in the ring; must stay legible at year-overview distance (pairs with LOD 5.2.9) and not z-fight with halos/blocks (`depthWrite: false` + `renderOrder` already handle this — keep).
- **Data flow notes:** Label text derives from `year`/`month` only; no event data.
- **Testing notes:** Each cluster has exactly one label mesh; texture is created once per build (not per frame); from the 5.2.2 camera all 12 labels are readable; after a rebuild, prior label textures are disposed (`renderer.info.memory.textures` doesn't grow per rebuild).
- **Performance notes:** Pre-rasterized = the cheap path (spec intent); the real risk is **texture leak** on rebuild (fix disposal) — and per-frame billboard is negligible.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Consider 2× canvas only when `devicePixelRatio > 1` (mirror §5.4 line 544).
- **Integration points:** §5.3 lines 509/517, 5.2.2, 5.2.9 (LOD), 5.2.10 (dispose completeness).
- **Status:** **Partial** (pre-rasterized ✓, centered-above ✓; **not billboarded**, per-month textures uncached, **label texture not disposed** in `_clear()`). **Next:** billboard if 5.2.2 orbits, add label disposal, optionally 2×/cache textures.

## 5.2.6 — AtomGlyph3D perimeter decorations (count, placement, determinism)

- **Purpose:** Reconcile the per-cluster ambient atoms to §5.3 line 512 / §5.5 line 561 — **2–4** per cluster positioned at the **perimeter / corners or edges** of the grid — and make placement **deterministic** so rebuilds don't reshuffle them.
- **Dependencies:** 5.2.3 (grid extents define the perimeter), 5.4 (AtomGlyph3D internals + shared geometry/material), 5.2.8 (rebuild cadence drives the determinism concern); §5.3 line 512, §5.5 lines 561–563.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Each cluster has **2–4** `AtomGlyph3D` decorations placed at the **edges/corners** of its day grid (not scattered through the center or behind it); placement is **deterministic** (seeded by `monthIndex`, not `Math.random()`) so a rebuild reproduces the same arrangement; atoms are decorative only (non-interactive, excluded from pickables, 5.6); they use the shared geometry/material path (detail in 5.4).
- **Implementation notes:** Current code (WordWeaverScene.js:880–895): `atomCount = 2 + ⌊Math.random()·3⌋` (= 2–4 ✓) but positions are `Math.random()` within a box (`±4.5` x, random −Y, z −2.5..−5) — that's **center-scattered and behind** the grid, and **non-deterministic**. Fixes: (1) derive count and positions from a **seed** (e.g. `mulberry32(monthIndex)` or a fixed per-index table) so rebuilds are stable; (2) place them along the grid **perimeter** — compute grid extents from `COL_SPACING`/`ROW_SPACING`/`weekCount` (5.2.3) and offset atoms just outside the corners/edges per §5.3 line 512; (3) keep using `AtomGlyph3D.createBackground` but defer the shared-`SphereGeometry`/shared-material optimization to 5.4 (§5.5 line 563); (4) ensure atoms are **not** added to pickables (5.6) and not driven by proximity focus. `update()` already ticks `bgAtoms` (1019) — keep.
