The live `update(delta)` (AtomGlyph3D.js:126-169) is richer: it spins the shell (`+= dt*0.35*rotMul`), spins the `ringGroup`, and additionally **scale-pulses each wave ring** (`Math.sin` wave/ripple, 142-153) and **orbits three electrons** (160-168) — motion well beyond "slow ring rotation". It is **delta-time-based** (frame-rate-independent — *good*, keep that property), with a `slowRotation` multiplier (0.18) for the background variant. 5.4.5 reconciles: keep delta-based timing but map to the spec's slow per-frame band, decide which "extra life" (wave pulse, electrons) survives as a deliberate enhancement vs. is trimmed for the ambient/year budget, and assign **per-ring randomized axes/speeds**.
- **`prefers-reduced-motion` is ignored — a hard accessibility gap.** §23.3 line 1665 and §27.6 line 1821 mandate that `AtomGlyph3D` rotation is **paused** under reduced-motion. `update()` (AtomGlyph3D.js:126) has **no** reduced-motion check and always animates; the scene's per-frame `for (const atom of this.bgAtoms) atom.update(delta)` (WordWeaverScene.js:1019) is unconditional. 5.4.6 honors the preference (skip/freeze updates), coordinated with the scene-level motion gate (a 5.1 carry).
- **Per-frame update of every atom; no LOD/cull for far clusters.** Frame-loop step 5 (§16.1 line 1306) is "Animate AtomGlyph3D rotations", but §1.4/§16 demand it not cost readability/perf. The scene updates **all** `bgAtoms` every frame (WordWeaverScene.js:1019) regardless of whether the cluster is near, far, or off-screen — and each update runs shell + 4-ring wave math + 3-electron orbits. With ~24–48 heavy atoms this is avoidable work. 5.4.7 gates updates by cluster proximity/visibility (reusing the per-cluster distance signal the scene already computes for DayBlock proximity, WordWeaverScene.js:1030) and/or freezes off-screen atoms.
- **Non-interactive invariant is not enforced against the (future) raycaster.** §5.5 line 562 = "No interactive function." The atom group is added straight to the cluster (`cluster.add(atom.group)`, WordWeaverScene.js:893) with a generic name `"atom-glyph-3d"` (AtomGlyph3D.js:16) and **no pick `userData`** — so it *currently* won't be picked, but 5.6's raycaster has no explicit exclusion contract yet, and DayBlock3D-style picking keys on `userData.dayBlock`/`name`. 5.4.8 makes the exclusion explicit (`raycast = () => {}` or a `userData.nonInteractive`/layer convention) so 5.6 can't accidentally pick decorations.
- **Dispose works today but becomes a double-free risk after sharing.** Each atom currently **owns** its geometry/materials, so `dispose()` (AtomGlyph3D.js:171-183, traverse → dispose all) and the scene's `bgAtoms` disposal (WordWeaverScene.js:1044-1047) are correct *now*. But once 5.4.3 moves geometry/materials to **shared module-level singletons**, this traverse-and-dispose-everything path would free the shared refs and break sibling atoms — exactly the 5.3.10 hazard. 5.4.9 splits owned vs. shared disposal (guarded) and adds a module-level shared-resource disposer; it also addresses the per-electron `eMat.clone()` leak (cloned materials must be tracked + disposed, or eliminated by sharing).
- **What's already right (keep):** delta-clamped update (`dt = clamp(delta, 0.001, 0.05)`, AtomGlyph3D.js:127) — frame-rate-safe ✓; the `slowRotation` flag + faint low-opacity background variant (`createBackground`, opacity ~0.1–0.2) read as *ambient*, not distracting ✓ (§1.4); `depthWrite: false` on the transparent shell/rings + `renderOrder` layering ✓ (correct transparency); `MeshStandardMaterial` for the rings ✓ (§5.5 line 558-559 material family); count **2–4** ✓ (`2 + 0..2`); a `dispose()` traverse exists ✓ (just needs the shared/owned split); atoms read **no model data** (pure decoration) ✓ (§5.5 line 562 non-interactive intent); `object3d` getter + `group.name` give a stable handle ✓.

---

## 5.4.1 — Canonical `AtomGlyph3D` class & spec-path reconciliation

- **Purpose:** Resolve the **three parallel atom implementations** into one canonical decorative `AtomGlyph3D` for the §5.5 cluster atom, and reconcile the spec's stated path (`src/wordweaver/AtomGlyph3D.js`, §11 line 146/1010/1963) with the live file (`src/wordweaver/timeline3d/AtomGlyph3D.js`) — so Phase 5 has a single, correctly-located decorative-atom class.
- **Dependencies:** 5.1 (canonical scene + module conventions), 5.2 (`populateMonthCluster` is the sole construction site), 5.3 (shared-resource pattern to mirror); §5.5 line 554, §11 line 146/1010/1963, §5.8 line 631.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Exactly **one** decorative-atom class is used by the year ring; its file location is reconciled with §11 (either move/rename the live `timeline3d/AtomGlyph3D.js` to the spec's `src/wordweaver/AtomGlyph3D.js`, or update §11 line 146/1010/1963 to the actual path — document the decision); the other two atoms (`AtomGlyph.js`, `WordWeaverAtomOrbits.js`) are explicitly dispositioned in 5.4.10 (retire / namespace / scope to the weave window) and do **not** leak into the cluster path; `createBackground` (or its successor) remains the single factory the scene calls.
- **Implementation notes:** Confirm via import graph that the year ring uses only `timeline3d/AtomGlyph3D.js` (WordWeaverScene.js:19, 884) and that `AtomGlyph.js` has **no** importer in the cluster path (grep). Recommend: keep `timeline3d/AtomGlyph3D.js` as the canonical class (it's the wired one) and **update the spec path** to match (cheaper + less risk than moving a file the satellite stack may also reference), OR move it to `src/wordweaver/AtomGlyph3D.js` if §11's tree is treated as normative — pick one and note it. Keep `createBackground` as the named factory but fold the spec geometry (5.4.2) and shared resources (5.4.3) into it.
- **Edge cases:** `timeline3d/AtomGlyph3D.js` ends with a side-effecting `if (typeof document !== "undefined") void import("../WordWeaverUI.js")` (AtomGlyph3D.js:186-188) — moving/renaming the file must preserve or relocate that import or it silently stops loading; the satellite weave's atom is a *different* class (`WordWeaverAtomOrbits`) — don't accidentally converge subsystems that should stay separate (5.4.10).
- **UI/UX considerations:** One canonical decoration keeps the year ring's ambient look consistent; divergent atom classes would drift visually over time.
- **Data flow notes:** Pure decoration; no model input. The only "flow" is scene → factory → cluster group.
- **Testing notes:** Grep confirms a single atom class imported in the year path; the spec path and the on-disk path agree (after the chosen fix); `AtomGlyph.js` has zero importers in the cluster path.
- **Performance notes:** Neutral (organizational); enables 5.4.2/5.4.3 to land in one place.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Same class both platforms; per-platform tuning is in 5.4.2/5.4.7.
- **Integration points:** §5.5 line 554, §11 line 146/1010/1963, 5.4.2, 5.4.3, 5.4.10 (other-atom disposition), 5.2.6 (the deferral this milestone answers).
- **Status:** **Divergent (path) + duplicated (3 classes).** Live atom is `timeline3d/AtomGlyph3D.js`; spec cites `src/wordweaver/AtomGlyph3D.js`; two other atom classes exist outside the cluster path. **Next:** pick `timeline3d/AtomGlyph3D.js` as canonical, reconcile the §11 path, disposition the other two in 5.4.10.

## 5.4.2 — Geometry & material to spec (`SphereGeometry(0.3,16,16)` + 2–3 `TorusGeometry` rings)

- **Purpose:** Reconcile the atom's geometry/material to §5.5 line 558-559 — a central `SphereGeometry(0.3, 16, 16)` on a `MeshStandardMaterial` with **slight emissive**, plus **2–3** `TorusGeometry` rings of varying radii — replacing the over-built 64×64 transmissive glass shell + four high-seg wave rings + three electrons, so the decoration matches its lightweight ambient role and budget (§1.4 line 88).
- **Dependencies:** 5.4.1 (canonical class), 5.4.3 (the shared refs this defines feed instancing), 5.1 (renderer/material conventions); §5.5 line 558-559, §1.4 line 88, §16 (budget).
- **Acceptance criteria:** Central sphere is `SphereGeometry(0.3, 16, 16)` (or the chosen base radius scaled — document if base differs from 0.3) with `MeshStandardMaterial` carrying a **slight** emissive (low `emissiveIntensity`, not the current `transmission:1.0`/`clearcoat:1.0` glass); rings are **2 or 3** `TorusGeometry` of varying radii with modest tube/segment counts appropriate to an ambient element (not 48×96); the expensive `transmission`/`thickness`/`ior`/`clearcoat` path is dropped (or gated to focused-only and off by default for decorations); the look stays faint/ambient (low opacity, §1.4). Any retained "premium" extras (wave-pulse, electrons) are an explicit, documented deviation budgeted in 5.4.5/5.4.7, not the default.
- **Implementation notes:** In `AtomGlyph3D` (AtomGlyph3D.js:25-103): replace `shellGeo = SphereGeometry(0.225, 64, 64)` + `MeshPhysicalMaterial{transmission:1.0,...}` with `SphereGeometry(0.3, 16, 16)` + `MeshStandardMaterial{ emissive, emissiveIntensity ~0.2-0.3, roughness, metalness, transparent, opacity, depthWrite:false }`; cut `ringSpecs` from **four** rings to **2–3** with lower segment counts (e.g. `TorusGeometry(r, t, 12, 32)`); **decide the electrons' fate** — §5.5 has no orbiting particles, so default to **removing** them (the other two atom classes already provide an orbital look in the weave window) or gate them off for the cluster decoration. Preserve the keepers: `depthWrite:false`, `renderOrder`, faint opacity, delta-clamped update.
- **Edge cases:** Dropping `transmission` changes the silhouette (no refraction) — verify the atom still reads against the dark §5.8 background (line 628) at low opacity; reducing torus segments too far makes rings visibly faceted up close (but these float behind the grid, so low seg is fine — confirm at focus distance); base radius 0.3 vs the live 0.225 changes apparent size — re-tune `createBackground` scale (5.4.4) accordingly.
- **UI/UX considerations:** The atom is *ambient depth*, not a hero object — a simpler, faint emissive sphere+rings is truer to §5.5/§1.4 than a costly glass orb that competes with the day tiles for attention.
- **Data flow notes:** Pure geometry/material; color comes from the per-color shared-material cache (5.4.3).
- **Testing notes:** Central sphere is `(0.3,16,16)`; ring count is 2–3; no `transmission`/`clearcoat` on the default decoration; `renderer.info` triangle/vertex count per atom drops substantially vs the 64×64 + 4×(48×96) + 3 electrons baseline; no electron spheres in the default cluster atom (or explicitly gated).
- **Performance notes:** This is a major per-atom triangle and material-feature reduction (esp. removing the transmission render-target pass) across ~24–48 atoms — the §1.4/§16 win; the *sharing* of these now-simpler geometries (5.4.3) compounds it.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Lower opacity / fewer rings (2) on mobile via `perfFlags.js`; desktop may keep 3 rings + the optional slight emissive pulse.
- **Integration points:** §5.5 line 558-559, §1.4 line 88, §5.8 line 628/631, 5.4.1, 5.4.3 (shares these geos/mats), 5.4.5 (animates these rings), `perfFlags.js`.
- **Status:** **Divergent (over-built).** Live = `SphereGeometry(0.225,64,64)` transmissive glass + **4** `TorusGeometry(48,96)` wave rings + 3 electrons. **Next:** reduce to `SphereGeometry(0.3,16,16)` + 2–3 low-seg tori on `MeshStandardMaterial` (slight emissive), drop/gate transmission and electrons.

## 5.4.3 — Shared `SphereGeometry` + two `TorusGeometry` refs, materials shared per-color (the 5.2.6 deferral)

- **Purpose:** Realize §5.5 line 563 + §5.3 line 516 — **all** atom instances share a **single `SphereGeometry` reference and two `TorusGeometry` references**, with **materials shared per color** — replacing the current per-instance allocation (fresh geometries every `new AtomGlyph3D()`, cloned electron materials). This is the explicit performance reconciliation **5.2.6 deferred to this milestone**.
- **Dependencies:** 5.4.2 (defines the geometries to share), 5.4.9 (the disposer that must *not* free shared refs), 5.3.10 (the shared-resource discipline this mirrors), 5.2.6 (the deferral); §5.5 line 563, §5.3 line 516.
- **Acceptance criteria:** A module-level shared-resource accessor (e.g. `getSharedAtomResources()`, mirroring `getSharedDayBlockResources`) returns **one** `SphereGeometry(0.3,16,16)` + **two** `TorusGeometry` refs (the 2–3 rings reuse the two torus geos by varying transform, not geometry) + a **per-color material cache** (a `Map<colorKey, MeshStandardMaterial>` so like-colored atoms share one material); `new AtomGlyph3D` allocates **no** geometry and **no** new material for an already-seen color; `renderer.info.memory.geometries`/`.programs` does **not** scale with atom count for the shared path; instancing is used where the same geometry+material recurs (an `InstancedMesh` per ring geometry across a cluster, *or* a documented shared-ref fallback if per-atom transforms make instancing impractical — state which).
- **Implementation notes:** Move the geometry/material creation out of the constructor (AtomGlyph3D.js:25-103) into a memoized module-level factory; have the constructor pull shared geos and a shared material keyed by color (replace the per-electron `eMat.clone()` at line 96 — if electrons survive 5.4.2, share their material too, or remove them). For the "two torus refs / 2–3 rings" reconciliation: use **two** distinct `TorusGeometry` instances and let the third ring (when present) **reuse** one of them at a different rotation/scale — so geometry count is fixed at sphere+2 tori regardless of atom count. Consider `InstancedMesh` per shared ring-geometry across all atoms in a cluster (or the whole ring) if the slow-rotation model allows a shared animated transform (5.4.5) — otherwise document that shared *refs* (not InstancedMesh) are the chosen optimization and why (per-atom independent ring axes/speeds, §5.5 line 560, can defeat naive instancing).
- **Edge cases:** Shared materials must not be mutated per-instance (e.g. per-atom opacity from the old `Math.random` path — fold opacity into a small set of shared materials or an instanced attribute, not a per-instance material clone); a shared geometry disposed by one atom's teardown would break all others (5.4.9 guards this); per-color cache must have a bounded key set (categories/palette colors, not arbitrary floats) so it doesn't grow unbounded.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Invisible to the user when correct; the payoff is steady frame time at year scale (§1.4 line 88).
- **Data flow notes:** Color key → shared material; geometry is color-independent (one set for all). No model data.
- **Testing notes:** Construct 48 atoms → geometry count rises by a small constant (sphere + 2 tori [+ optional electron]) **not** by 48×; material count equals the number of **distinct colors**, not the atom count; disposing one atom leaves siblings intact (5.4.9); with InstancedMesh (if chosen), draw calls for atoms are O(ring-geometries) not O(atoms).
- **Performance notes:** This is the headline 5.2.6/§5.3-line-516 win — collapses dozens of geometries/materials to a handful and (optionally) atom draw calls to a few; combined with 5.4.2's lighter geometry it makes the ambient atoms effectively free.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Sharing benefits both; mobile additionally caps ring count (5.4.2) and atom count (5.4.4).
- **Integration points:** §5.5 line 563, §5.3 line 516, 5.2.6 (deferral resolved here), 5.3.10 (pattern), 5.4.2 (defines geos), 5.4.9 (guarded dispose), 5.4.7 (instanced update if chosen).
- **Status:** **Missing (the explicit 5.2.6 deferral).** Each `new AtomGlyph3D()` allocates fresh shell + 4 ring geos + clones electron materials; no sharing, no instancing. **Next:** module-level shared `SphereGeometry` + 2 `TorusGeometry` refs + per-color material cache (mirror `getSharedDayBlockResources`); instance or document the shared-ref fallback.

## 5.4.4 — Deterministic placement at cluster corners/edges (2–4 per cluster)

- **Purpose:** Reconcile placement to §5.5 line 561 / §5.3 line 512 — **2–4** atoms positioned at the **corners or edges (perimeter)** of the day grid — replacing the current `Math.random` count/scale/opacity/position that scatters atoms at random interior/behind positions and changes on every rebuild. Closes the determinism + perimeter concern **5.2.6 flagged and deferred**.
- **Dependencies:** 5.2.1 (cluster grid dimensions/footprint define the perimeter), 5.2.6 (the deferral), 5.2.8 (full-rebuild path — deterministic placement makes rebuilds visually stable), 5.4.2 (base size); §5.5 line 561, §5.3 line 512.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Atom **count** is a fixed 2–4 derived **deterministically** (e.g. from `monthIndex` or a seeded value), not `Math.random`; **positions** are computed from the cluster's grid bounds to sit at **corners/edges of the perimeter** (e.g. the four corners for 4, two opposing corners for 2), at a slight +Z so they read as floating *around* the cluster (not buried behind at z=−2.5…−6); **scale/opacity** are stable constants (or deterministic per-corner), not random; re-running `populateMonthCluster` for the same month yields the **same** atom layout (reproducible — supports caching and stable visuals across 5.2.8 rebuilds).
- **Implementation notes:** In `populateMonthCluster` (WordWeaverScene.js:880-895): replace `atomCount = 2 + Math.floor(Math.random()*3)` with a deterministic count; replace the random `position`/`scale`/`opacity` with perimeter anchors computed from the grid extent (columns span `(col-3)*COL_SPACING`, rows span `0…-(weekCount-1)*ROW_SPACING` — derive min/max X and Y, place atoms just outside those bounds at corners/edge midpoints); drop the `createBackground` default `z=-6` shove (AtomGlyph3D.js:122) in favor of a small consistent +Z offset so atoms float *near* the cluster plane perimeter, per §5.3 line 512 "floating around the cluster perimeter". Keep count in 2–4. If a touch of organic variety is desired, derive it deterministically from `monthIndex` (e.g. a hash), not `Math.random`.
- **Edge cases:** Clusters with 4 vs 6 week-rows have different heights — perimeter math must use the actual `weekCount` extent (5.2.3), not a fixed value; corner atoms must not overlap neighboring clusters at ring radius 80 (coordinate with 5.2.1 spacing); at the spec's larger radius the perimeter offset must scale with the cluster, not be absolute; §5.8 line 631 *also* wants some atoms **outside** clusters in open scene space — keep that as a separate, small ambient set (5.4.10) distinct from these per-cluster perimeter atoms.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Perimeter placement frames the month without occluding day tiles; deterministic layout means the scene looks the same each visit (a calmer, more trustworthy space — §1.4) rather than reshuffling on every data change.
- **Data flow notes:** Inputs = cluster grid bounds (from 5.2 layout) + `monthIndex`; output = fixed atom transforms. No model/random input.
- **Testing notes:** Same month → identical atom positions across two builds (snapshot the transforms); atoms sit at/just outside the grid perimeter (not interior, not far behind); count is within 2–4; positions scale correctly for 4- vs 6-row months and at radius 80.
- **Performance notes:** Determinism enables layout caching (5.2.8) and removes per-rebuild churn; negligible cost itself.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Mobile may cap at 2 atoms/cluster (fewer perimeter anchors); desktop up to 4.
- **Integration points:** §5.5 line 561, §5.3 line 512, §5.8 line 631 (separate out-of-cluster ambient set → 5.4.10), 5.2.1/5.2.3 (grid bounds/week rows), 5.2.6 (deferral), 5.2.8 (rebuild stability), 5.4.2 (base size).
- **Status:** **Divergent (non-deterministic, wrong region).** `Math.random` count/scale/opacity/position scatters atoms at random interior/behind spots, reshuffling every rebuild. **Next:** deterministic 2–4 count + perimeter-corner/edge anchors from the grid bounds, stable scale/opacity, small +Z float.

## 5.4.5 — Slow ring rotation on randomized axes (0.002–0.008 rad/frame)

- **Purpose:** Reconcile the animation to §5.5 line 560 / §23.2 line 1656 — each ring rotates at a **distinct slow speed (0.002–0.008 rad/frame)** around a **randomized axis**, linear and continuous — while preserving the live code's frame-rate-independent (delta-based) timing, and deciding which of the current "premium" extras (wave-pulse scaling, orbiting electrons) survive.
- **Dependencies:** 5.4.2 (the 2–3 rings being rotated), 5.4.3 (shared/instanced transforms must allow per-ring independent rotation — or this constrains the instancing choice), 5.4.6 (reduced-motion pauses this), 5.4.7 (only near/visible atoms update); §5.5 line 560, §23.2 line 1656.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Each ring rotates continuously at a **distinct** slow rate; the rate is specified as the spec's per-frame band **0.002–0.008 rad/frame** but implemented **delta-based** (rate-per-second = per-frame × ~60, applied as `+= ratePerSec * dt`) so it's frame-rate-independent (the live `dt`-clamp at AtomGlyph3D.js:127 is kept); each ring's rotation **axis is randomized** (deterministically seeded per 5.4.4) and distinct from its siblings; the motion is **linear** (no easing, §23.2); any retained wave-pulse/electron motion is an explicit documented enhancement gated for desktop/near-only (5.4.7), not the §5.5 default.
- **Implementation notes:** In `update(delta)` (AtomGlyph3D.js:126-169): keep the `dt` clamp; set each ring's per-frame increment from a per-ring constant in the 0.002–0.008 band (converted to per-second: ~0.12–0.48 rad/s) applied as `ring.rotateOnAxis(axis, ratePerSec * dt)` with a **randomized (seeded) axis** per ring; **decide on the extras** — the wave-pulse scaling (142-153) and 3 electron orbits (160-168) exceed §5.5's "slowly rotating rings" and add per-frame cost across ~24–48 atoms: default to **removing/disabling** them for the cluster atom (keep them, if at all, only for the focused/near tier on desktop, 5.4.7). Preserve `slowRotation`'s intent (background atoms are *slow*) by living in the low end of the band.
- **Edge cases:** Per-frame constants (0.002–0.008 rad) assume 60fps — the delta-based conversion must hold the same *visual* speed at 30/120fps (verify); randomized axes must be normalized unit vectors; if 5.4.3 chose `InstancedMesh`, per-ring independent rotation may require per-instance matrices updated each frame (a cost trade — document, or fall back to shared-ref non-instanced for animated atoms); reduced-motion (5.4.6) must cleanly freeze without snapping.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Slow, distinct-axis rotation gives "ambient life" (§5.5 line 562) without drawing the eye; fast or synchronized rotation would look mechanical and distracting (§1.4).
- **Data flow notes:** Time/delta in → ring transforms out; axes/speeds seeded from 5.4.4's deterministic per-atom seed.
- **Testing notes:** Each ring rotates at a different rate within 0.002–0.008 rad/frame-equivalent; visual speed is identical at 30/60/120fps (delta-based); axes differ per ring; no easing; with extras removed, the per-atom `update` cost drops to ~2–3 ring rotations.
- **Performance notes:** Trimming wave-pulse + electron orbits removes the bulk of per-frame atom math; the remaining 2–3 `rotateOnAxis` calls are cheap; gating to near/visible atoms (5.4.7) bounds the total.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Mobile: 2 rings, slow end of the band, no extras; desktop: up to 3 rings, optional focused-tier extras.
- **Integration points:** §5.5 line 560, §23.2 line 1656, 5.4.2 (rings), 5.4.3 (instancing vs per-ring rotation trade), 5.4.6 (pause), 5.4.7 (update gating).
- **Status:** **Partial / over-rich.** Delta-based timing ✓ (keep), but motion is shell-spin + 4-ring wave-pulse + 3-electron orbit, not "2–3 rings slowly rotating on randomized axes"; speeds not mapped to the 0.002–0.008 band. **Next:** per-ring randomized-axis slow rotation in-band (delta-based), trim/gate the extras.

## 5.4.6 — Honor `prefers-reduced-motion` (pause rotation)

- **Purpose:** Satisfy §23.3 line 1665 + §27.6 line 1821 — under `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`, **`AtomGlyph3D` rotation is paused** (the 3D mode's continuous motion is fully stilled) — closing a hard accessibility gap (the live `update` always animates).
- **Dependencies:** 5.4.5 (the motion being paused), 5.1 (scene-level reduced-motion / animate-loop gate — a 5.1 carry), 5.4.7 (the update path where the gate is applied); §23.3 line 1665, §27.6 line 1821, §23.3 line 1662-1666 (reduced-motion contract).
- **Acceptance criteria:** When `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` is active, atoms render **static** (no ring rotation, no wave-pulse, no electron orbit) — `update()` is a no-op or is not called; the preference is observed **live** (a `matchMedia` change listener re-freezes/resumes without reload); resuming motion when the preference clears does not snap or jump (atoms resume from their current orientation); this coordinates with the scene-level motion gate (camera float etc. also pause per §27.6) rather than being a one-off.
- **Implementation notes:** Prefer a **single scene-level** reduced-motion flag (set from `window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)')` with a `change` listener, owned by the canonical scene from 5.1) that the per-frame loop checks before `for (const atom of bgAtoms) atom.update(delta)` (WordWeaverScene.js:1019) — skip the loop when reduced. Alternatively (or additionally) guard inside `AtomGlyph3D.update` so the class is safe regardless of caller. Ensure no first-frame snap: on resume, just continue advancing `_elapsed` from where it paused (don't reset phase).
- **Edge cases:** The preference can change at runtime (OS toggle) — the listener must update the flag live; SSR/no-`window` guard (the file already guards `typeof document`); reduced-motion should also pause the optional electron/wave extras (5.4.5); interaction-driven camera moves (5.5) under reduced-motion snap per §23.3 line 1664 — keep atom freeze consistent with that.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Vestibular-safety is non-negotiable (§27.6); a still atom field is the correct reduced-motion presentation (depth without motion).
- **Data flow notes:** `matchMedia` → scene reduced-motion flag → per-frame gate. No model data.
- **Testing notes:** With reduced-motion on, atoms do not rotate over time (snapshot two frames → identical transforms); toggling the OS preference live freezes/resumes without reload; resuming doesn't jump.
- **Performance notes:** Bonus — reduced-motion also skips all atom update cost (free win on that path).
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Both honor the OS/browser preference identically.
- **Integration points:** §23.3 line 1665, §27.6 line 1821, 5.1 (scene motion gate), 5.4.5 (paused motion), 5.4.7 (gate location).
- **Status:** **Missing (accessibility gap).** `update()` always animates; scene update loop is unconditional. **Next:** scene-level `prefers-reduced-motion` flag (live `matchMedia` listener) gating the atom update loop; safe resume.

## 5.4.7 — Frame-loop update wiring, proximity/visibility gating, LOD

- **Purpose:** Reconcile the per-frame update to §16.1 line 1306 (frame-loop step 5 "Animate AtomGlyph3D rotations") **and** §1.4 line 88 (must not cost perf) — update only **near/visible** atoms (and optionally enable the heavier extras only for the focused/near tier), rather than animating every one of ~24–48 atoms (with full shell+4-ring+3-electron math) every frame regardless of distance/visibility.
- **Dependencies:** 5.4.5 (the motion being gated), 5.4.6 (reduced-motion is the first gate), 5.4.3 (instanced vs per-atom update affects how gating is applied), 5.2 (per-cluster distance/visibility signal — the scene already computes per-block camera distance at WordWeaverScene.js:1030); §16.1 line 1306, §1.4 line 88, §16 (LOD/budget), §16.3 (distance-tiered detail, mirrors 5.3.8).
- **Acceptance criteria:** Atoms in **off-screen or far** clusters are **not** updated each frame (skipped or frozen via a per-cluster proximity/frustum check, reusing the signal the scene already computes); near/visible atoms animate per 5.4.5; the optional extras (wave-pulse, electrons — if retained from 5.4.5) run **only** for the focused/near tier on capable platforms; total per-frame atom work scales with **visible** atoms, not total atom count; the update is invoked from the canonical scene's frame loop as the §16.1 step-5 "Animate AtomGlyph3D rotations" stage.
- **Implementation notes:** Gate the `for (const atom of this.bgAtoms) atom.update(delta)` loop (WordWeaverScene.js:1019): compute or reuse the per-cluster camera distance / frustum result (the scene computes per-block distance at 1030 — lift to per-cluster) and `continue` for far/off-screen clusters; optionally pass a `detail` hint to `atom.update(delta, { detail })` so near atoms get extras and far atoms get rotation-only (ties to 5.4.5's extras gating). If 5.4.3 chose `InstancedMesh` per ring, gating becomes "update the instance matrices only for visible clusters". Keep the `dt` clamp.
- **Edge cases:** A cluster crossing the near/far boundary must not pop (atoms were frozen, then jump) — either keep advancing a cheap phase for just-off-screen clusters or accept a freeze that resumes smoothly (atoms are subtle enough that a frozen far atom is invisible anyway); frustum culling already removes off-screen *rendering*, but `update()` still runs CPU-side without this gate — that's the waste being removed; ensure the gate doesn't fight the §16 culling that 5.2.9 left unverified.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Invisible when correct; preserves frame budget for the day tiles and camera (§1.4), which are what the user actually reads/drives.
- **Data flow notes:** Per-cluster distance/visibility → update gate; no model data.
- **Testing notes:** With the camera on one cluster, `update` runs for ~that cluster's atoms only (instrument a counter); panning across the ring keeps per-frame atom-update count bounded (~visible clusters × 2–4), not ~24–48; frame time with the full ring populated stays within the §16.4 budget.
- **Performance notes:** Combined with 5.4.2 (lighter geometry) + 5.4.3 (shared/instanced) + 5.4.5 (trimmed motion), this bounds ambient-atom cost to visible work — the §1.4/§16 guarantee.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Mobile: rotation-only, tighter visibility gate, no extras; desktop: focused-tier extras allowed.
- **Integration points:** §16.1 line 1306, §1.4 line 88, §16.3 (LOD tiers, parallels 5.3.8), 5.2 (distance/frustum signal), 5.2.9 (culling), 5.4.3/5.4.5/5.4.6.
- **Status:** **Missing (gating) / present (wiring).** The scene *does* call `atom.update(delta)` every frame (§16.1 step-5 satisfied) but for **all** atoms with no proximity/visibility/LOD gate. **Next:** gate the update loop by per-cluster proximity/frustum, run extras only near/focused, keep total work ∝ visible atoms.

## 5.4.8 — Enforce the non-interactive invariant (raycaster exclusion)

- **Purpose:** Guarantee §5.5 line 562 — atoms have **no interactive function** — by making them explicitly **unpickable**, so 5.6's raycaster cannot hover/select/route on a decoration (today they merely *happen* not to be picked; there's no enforced contract).
- **Dependencies:** 5.6 (the raycaster that must honor this — 5.4.8 is the contract 5.6 consumes), 5.3 (DayBlock3D's pick convention — atoms must be distinguishable from it), 5.4.1 (canonical class); §5.5 line 562, §5.7 (interaction/raycast), §11 line 1010 (atoms are leaf decorations).
- **Acceptance criteria:** Atom meshes are excluded from raycasting via an explicit mechanism — either `mesh.raycast = () => {}` on the atom's meshes, a dedicated non-interactive `Layer` the raycaster doesn't test, or a `userData.nonInteractive = true` flag the 5.6 raycast filter checks; atoms carry **no** pick handle resembling DayBlock3D's (`userData.dayBlock`, `name = "day-block-*"`); a raycast through an atom that sits in front of a day tile still selects the **tile**, never the atom; the contract is documented so 5.6 implements the matching filter.
- **Implementation notes:** In `AtomGlyph3D` construction (AtomGlyph3D.js): set `raycast = () => {}` on the shell/ring/electron meshes (cheapest, self-contained) **or** tag `group.userData.nonInteractive = true` and have 5.6's raycaster pre-filter; keep the generic `group.name = "atom-glyph-3d"` but ensure 5.6 keys picking on the DayBlock handle (`userData.dayBlock`), not on broad `intersectObjects(scene, true)` without a filter. Coordinate the chosen mechanism with 5.6 so both sides agree.
- **Edge cases:** If 5.4.3 uses `InstancedMesh`, the whole instanced object must be excluded (one `raycast` no-op on the InstancedMesh); if the raycaster uses `intersectObjects(..., recursive=true)` it will still descend into atom children unless they're individually excluded or filtered — prefer the layer/flag filter at the raycaster for robustness; a future "click atom for easter-egg" idea would violate §5.5 line 562 — keep it non-interactive.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Decorations that intercept clicks/hovers would be a frustrating dead-zone over the calendar — the invariant protects the interaction model (§5.7).
- **Data flow notes:** No input/output; purely removes atoms from the pick set.
- **Testing notes:** Programmatic raycast aimed at an atom in front of a day tile returns the **tile** (or nothing for the atom), never the atom; hovering an atom produces no DayBlock hover state (5.3.5/5.6); the atom group has no `userData.dayBlock`.
- **Performance notes:** Excluding atoms also **shrinks the raycast set** (fewer objects tested per pointer move) — a minor 5.6 win.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Identical; touch and mouse both route past atoms to tiles.
- **Integration points:** §5.5 line 562, §5.7 (5.6 raycaster filter), 5.3 (DayBlock pick handle), 5.4.1, 5.4.3 (InstancedMesh exclusion).
- **Status:** **Implicit / unenforced.** Atoms currently aren't picked (no pick handle) but there's no explicit exclusion and 5.6's raycaster contract doesn't yet guarantee it. **Next:** `raycast`-no-op / `nonInteractive` flag / dedicated layer + a matching 5.6 filter; document the contract.

## 5.4.9 — Dispose discipline: owned vs. shared, no double-free, no material-clone leak

- **Purpose:** Make atom teardown correct **after** 5.4.3 introduces shared geometry/materials — owned per-instance resources are disposed, **shared** singletons are **never** freed by an instance, and the per-electron material clones (if any survive) don't leak — mirroring the 5.3.10 shared-resource-disposer lesson.
- **Dependencies:** 5.4.3 (defines what is shared vs owned), 5.3.10 (the pattern), 5.2.8/5.2.10 (the cluster rebuild/dispose path that calls atom dispose — WordWeaverScene.js:1044-1047); §5.5 line 563 (sharing implies a sharing-aware lifecycle), §16 (no GPU-resource leaks).
- **Acceptance criteria:** `AtomGlyph3D.dispose()` frees only **instance-owned** objects (the `Group`, any per-instance attributes/instanced buffers) and **does not** dispose the shared `SphereGeometry`/`TorusGeometry`/per-color materials (5.4.3); a **module-level disposer** (e.g. `disposeSharedAtomResources()`) frees the shared singletons exactly once at scene teardown (5.1 dispose); the current traverse-and-dispose-everything (AtomGlyph3D.js:171-183) is replaced with the owned/shared split; any retained `eMat.clone()` (AtomGlyph3D.js:96) materials are tracked and disposed (or eliminated by sharing in 5.4.3); rebuilding a cluster (5.2.8) and disposing the old atoms leaves **siblings and shared refs intact** (no GPU-resource growth across rebuilds).
- **Implementation notes:** Rework `dispose()` (171-183): stop the blanket `traverse → geometry?.dispose()/material?.dispose()` (that would free shared refs after 5.4.3); instead remove the group from its parent and dispose only owned buffers; keep a `_owned` list if any per-instance resources exist. Add `disposeSharedAtomResources()` to the shared-resource module (5.4.3) called from the canonical scene's `dispose()` (5.1). In the scene's `_clear`/year-layout dispose (WordWeaverScene.js:1044-1047), the per-atom `dispose()` stays but now only frees owned bits; shared teardown happens once at scene dispose. If electrons are removed in 5.4.2, the `eMat.clone()` leak disappears; if kept, share or track+dispose them.
- **Edge cases:** Disposing shared geometry while atoms still reference it (wrong order) breaks rendering — the module disposer runs only at full scene teardown, never on per-cluster rebuild; double scene init/dispose must not double-free the shared singletons (idempotent disposer + recreate-on-next-get); `InstancedMesh` (5.4.3) instance buffers are owned and must be disposed even though the geometry is shared.
- **UI/UX considerations:** None directly; correctness prevents memory growth / context loss over long 3D sessions (§16).
- **Data flow notes:** Lifecycle only.
- **Testing notes:** Build → dispose → rebuild a cluster repeatedly; `renderer.info.memory.geometries`/`.textures`/material count returns to baseline and does **not** grow across cycles; after per-atom dispose, shared geos/materials still valid for siblings; full scene dispose frees the shared singletons exactly once.
- **Performance notes:** Prevents the leak that per-instance allocation + naive dispose would otherwise cause across 5.2.8 rebuilds.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Identical; matters most on long-lived mobile sessions.
- **Integration points:** §5.5 line 563, §16, 5.4.3 (shared/owned split), 5.3.10 (pattern), 5.2.8/5.2.10 (rebuild/dispose), 5.1 (scene dispose calls the module disposer).
- **Status:** **Correct-now / future-hazard.** `dispose()` traverses and frees everything — fine while each atom owns its resources, but a **double-free** once 5.4.3 shares geometry/materials; `eMat.clone()` is a per-electron material allocation. **Next:** owned/shared dispose split + a once-only module-level shared disposer; track or eliminate cloned electron materials.

## 5.4.10 — Decoration close-out: disposition the parallel atoms, out-of-cluster ambient set, spec-path & bus reconciliation

- **Purpose:** Close Phase 5's decorative-atom thread: formally disposition the **two non-canonical** atom classes (`AtomGlyph.js`, `WordWeaverAtomOrbits.js`), add the §5.8 line 631 **out-of-cluster** ambient atoms, finalize the §11 path reconciliation, and record that atoms (correctly) emit/consume **nothing** on the event bus — so the decoration system is converged, documented, and consistent with the Phase-4/5 single-source/single-bus discipline.
- **Dependencies:** 5.4.1 (canonical class chosen), 5.4.4 (per-cluster placement — the out-of-cluster set is separate), all prior 5.4 tasks; §5.8 line 631, §11 line 146/1010/1963, §13.2 (event catalog — atoms are absent by design).
- **Acceptance criteria:** `AtomGlyph.js` (gold) and `WordWeaverAtomOrbits.js` (pink weave) are each explicitly dispositioned — **retire** the unused one(s), or **namespace/document** them as the *satellite-weave* decoration distinct from the §5.5 cluster atom (confirm `WordWeaverAtomOrbits` stays scoped to `weaveGroup`/`getActivePalette().atomOrbits` and is **not** used in the year ring); the §5.8 line 631 "subtle floating `AtomGlyph3D` elements **outside** month clusters" are added as a small, deterministic ambient set in open scene space (reusing the canonical class + shared resources + reduced-motion gate), distinct from 5.4.4's per-cluster perimeter atoms; the §11 path (line 146/1010/1963) and the on-disk path agree (5.4.1 decision applied); it is documented that `AtomGlyph3D` **does not emit or consume** any §13.2 events (non-interactive, model-independent) — *correct*, and not a gap to fix.
- **Implementation notes:** Grep importers of `AtomGlyph.js` — if none in the live app, retire it (or fold any worth-keeping look into the canonical class); leave `WordWeaverAtomOrbits` as the documented weave-window decoration (it's a different subsystem — don't converge it into the cluster atom, just label the boundary). Add the out-of-cluster ambient atoms in the canonical scene (5.1) at deterministic open-space positions outside the ring footprint, faint and slow, honoring 5.4.6/5.4.7. Add a short note to the §13.2 carry-forward that decorations are intentionally bus-silent (unlike the Phase-2/§13.2 model-driven objects).
- **Edge cases:** Retiring `AtomGlyph.js` must not break a lazy/dynamic import (grep for string-based imports too); the out-of-cluster set must not collide with clusters at radius 80 or drift into the camera's near plane (§5.6/5.1.4 fov/near tuning); the §5.8 background atoms share the reduced-motion/visibility gates (don't animate when off-screen or reduced-motion).
- **UI/UX considerations:** A small amount of open-space ambient depth (§5.8) makes the void around the ring feel intentional, not empty — without competing with the clusters; converging the duplicate classes prevents future visual drift.
- **Data flow notes:** None (decorations are model- and bus-independent — the documented invariant).
- **Testing notes:** `AtomGlyph.js` has zero live importers (or is retired); `WordWeaverAtomOrbits` appears only in the weave path, never the year ring; out-of-cluster atoms exist in open space, deterministic, and obey reduced-motion/visibility gates; no §13.2 events originate from atoms.
- **Performance notes:** The out-of-cluster set is small and shares the same resources/gates — negligible; retiring a dead class is a maintenance win.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Mobile may omit the out-of-cluster ambient set entirely; desktop includes a few.
- **Integration points:** §5.8 line 631, §11 line 146/1010/1963, §13.2 (bus-silent by design), 5.4.1 (canonical/path), 5.4.4 (per-cluster vs out-of-cluster), 5.4.6/5.4.7 (gates), 5.1 (out-of-cluster atoms live on the canonical scene).
- **Status:** **Open (convergence + additions).** Three atom classes coexist; the §5.8 out-of-cluster ambient set is absent; the §11 path is unreconciled; bus-silence is correct but undocumented. **Next:** disposition `AtomGlyph.js`/`WordWeaverAtomOrbits.js`, add the deterministic out-of-cluster ambient atoms, apply the 5.4.1 path fix, document the intentional bus-silence.

<!-- source: phase-05-milestone-5.5-camera.md -->

# Phase 5 — Milestone 5.5: Camera System (Expanded)

**Goal:** Specify the WordWeaver 3D camera (§5.6): a `PerspectiveCamera` (FOV 60°, near 0.1, far 2000, aspect on resize) driven by `OrbitControls` on desktop and a **custom touch handler** on mobile, with three canonical poses (year overview `(0, 60, 120)` → origin; month focus ~20 units out; day focus close-framed on a `DayBlock3D`), **lerp-based transitions** (`camera.position.lerpTo(target, 0.08)` + `controls.target.lerpTo(lookAt, 0.08)` per frame, snap when distance < 0.01), and a focus API — `focusOnDay`, `focusOnMonth`, `focusOnToday`, `focusOnNextEvent`, `focusOnCategory`. This milestone is grounded against the **already-existing** inline camera logic inside [`WordWeaverScene.js`](../../src/wordweaver/WordWeaverScene.js) (`_frameYearCamera`, `focusOnMonth`, `focusOnDay`, `_startCameraFocus`, `_updateCameraFocus`), the **standalone** [`CameraController.js`](../../src/calendar/CameraController.js) used by the 2D-wall `CalendarApp`, the desktop fly-input in [`WordWeaverTimelineViewport.js`](../../src/wordweaver/WordWeaverTimelineViewport.js) and [`WordWeaverChrome.js`](../../src/wordweaver/WordWeaverChrome.js), and the mobile overlay [`WordWeaverMobileControls.js`](../../src/wordweaver/WordWeaverMobileControls.js) — so every task reconciles built / gap / next-step, not greenfield. It owns the camera **intrinsics, poses, transitions, controls, and focus API**; it does **not** cover the raycaster / hover / click routing (§5.7 → 5.6), the day tile (5.3), the ring/cluster layout that *supplies* world positions (5.2), or decorations (5.4 — which must stay world-fixed as the camera moves, 5.4.5's contract).

**Spec alignment:** §5.6 line 565-602 (camera type `PerspectiveCamera` FOV 60° near 0.1 far 2000 line 567; `OrbitControls` desktop with left-drag orbit / right-drag pan / wheel zoom / **WASD fly** / **Q-E elevate-descend** / **R reset** lines 569-575; custom touch handler mobile — single-drag orbit, pinch zoom, two-finger pan, thumb-slide fly, fly/descend buttons lines 577-583; poses year `(0,60,120)`→origin, month ~20 out, day close-framed lines 585-588; transitions `lerpTo(...,0.08)` per frame + snap < 0.01 lines 590-595; focus API `focusOnDay`/`focusOnMonth`/`focusOnToday`/`focusOnNextEvent`/`focusOnCategory` lines 597-602), §5.6 line 487 ("Create `PerspectiveCamera` (FOV 60, near 0.1, far 1000)" — the scene-init restatement; note the **far-plane disagreement** 1000 vs 2000, both > the live 80, ties to 5.1.4), §11 line 147 (Camera = `PerspectiveCamera` + `OrbitControls` desktop + custom touch mobile), §11 line 1011 (the architecture tree lists `CameraController` under the scene), §16.1 line 1302 (frame-loop step 1 = "Update `OrbitControls` (desktop) or custom touch controls"), §13.2 line 1418 ("User navigates in 3D → updates `focusedDate`, emits `dayFocused`/`monthFocused` → camera animates"), §13.2 line 436 (both `WordWeaverScene` and `Calendar2D` should listen `navigateTo`), §13.2 line 1101-1102 / 1278 (`focusOnDay(3,14)` / `focusOnMonth(5)` data-flow exemplars), §23.2 line 1655 ("Camera navigate | focusOnDay/Month | continuous | lerp factor 0.08"), §23.3 line 1664 (under `prefers-reduced-motion`, **camera transitions snap immediately** — lerp factor set to 1 for one frame), §5.7 line 624 (Escape returns camera month-overview → year-overview — interaction-side, coordinated with 5.6), §1.4 line 88 (motion never costs readability/perf).

**Sequencing:** Fifth milestone of Phase 5. It depends on 5.1's canonical scene (the camera + controls are created in scene init — `PerspectiveCamera` at WordWeaverScene.js:53, `OrbitControls` at :71) and on 5.2's layout (focus targets come from `monthClusters[i]` world positions and `findDayBlock(m,d)` — WordWeaverScene.js:215-231). It feeds 5.6 (the raycaster turns a pick into a `focusOnDay`/`focusOnMonth` call — WordWeaverScene.js:690 already does this; 5.6 owns the pick, 5.5 owns the resulting camera move) and coordinates with 5.4.5 (decorations stay world-fixed; the camera must not make atoms appear to track the user). It carries the two Phase-5 recurring threads: the **duplicate-renderer / parallel-stack** pattern — here **three** camera/movement subsystems (inline `_cameraFocus` in `WordWeaverScene`, the standalone `CameraController.js` on the 2D wall, and the fly-movement in `WordWeaverTimelineViewport.js`, plus **two** keyboard input paths) — and the **§13.2 event-bus gap** (focus methods emit no `dayFocused`/`monthFocused` and consume no `navigateTo`, the recurring Phase-4/5 finding).

---

## Current implementation status (reconciliation)

A working camera **already exists and is live in the year ring** — `PerspectiveCamera` + `OrbitControls`, a year-overview framer, `focusOnMonth`/`focusOnDay`, an eased focus animation, desktop WASD/Q-E fly, and a custom mobile overlay. But the **intrinsics diverge** (FOV 42 not 60, far 80 not 2000), the **poses differ** from the spec numbers, the **transition model is time-based easing not the spec's per-frame factor-0.08 lerp**, the focus API is **incomplete** (no `focusOnToday`/`focusOnNextEvent`/`focusOnCategory`, and `focusOnDay` doesn't open the day panel), there are **three parallel camera/movement subsystems and two keyboard input paths**, reduced-motion is **not honored**, and focus emits **no §13.2 events**. This is an upgrade/reconcile + converge milestone, not greenfield.

- **Three parallel camera/movement subsystems; the §11 tree's `CameraController` isn't the one driving the ring.** §11 line 1011 lists `CameraController` under the scene, but the **live year-ring camera move** is **inline** in `WordWeaverScene` (`_startCameraFocus`/`_updateCameraFocus`, WordWeaverScene.js:241-270) — a time-based eased `lerpVectors`. The **standalone** [`CameraController.js`](../../src/calendar/CameraController.js) (`zoomToDay`/`zoomToOverview`/`restoreCamera`, promise-based `easeInOutCubic`, durations 900/800ms) is wired to the **2D-wall** `CalendarApp` (CalendarApp.js:27, 97), **not** the year ring. A **third** subsystem — fly translation — lives in `WordWeaverTimelineViewport.js` (:36-73, moves both `camera.position` and `controls.target`). 5.5.1 picks the canonical camera class/path and reconciles §11; the others are dispositioned (keep `CameraController` for the wall, fold fly-movement into the canonical camera).
- **Intrinsics diverge: FOV 42 (not 60), far 80 (not 2000/1000).** §5.6 line 567 wants FOV 60°, near 0.1, far 2000 (line 487 says far 1000 — an internal spec disagreement to settle); the live camera is `new THREE.PerspectiveCamera(42, 1, 0.1, 80)` (WordWeaverScene.js:53). **Far = 80 is the 5.1.4 ring-clip bug** (at the spec's ring radius ~80 the back of the ring sits up to ~160 units out and is clipped). Near 0.1 ✓ and aspect-on-resize ✓ (`camera.aspect = w/h; updateProjectionMatrix()`, WordWeaverScene.js:634-635). 5.5.2 reconciles FOV/far and settles the 1000-vs-2000 spec conflict.
- **Poses differ from the spec numbers; two competing "home" concepts.** §5.6 line 586-588 wants year overview `(0, 60, 120)` → origin, month ~20 units out, day close-framed. Live: `_frameYearCamera` sets `(0, 18, 42)` → origin with `maxDistance 120` (WordWeaverScene.js:204-208) — much nearer/lower than `(0,60,120)`; `focusOnMonth` uses **distance 14** (not ~20) at height 2.5 (WordWeaverScene.js:219); `focusOnDay` uses distance 5.5 / height 1.2 (WordWeaverScene.js:233). There are **two** home notions: `_cameraHome`/`_targetHome` (WordWeaverScene.js:57-58, `(0,2.4,8.2)`→`(0,1.1,0)`, the *embed* default) **and** `_frameYearCamera`'s year-overview pose — they don't agree on what "overview" is. 5.5.3 reconciles the three poses to the spec and unifies the home concept.
- **Transition model is fixed-duration easing, not the spec's per-frame factor-0.08 lerp + snap.** §5.6 line 590-595 / §23.2 line 1655 specify per-frame `lerpTo(target, 0.08)` on both `camera.position` and `controls.target`, with a **snap when distance < 0.01**. The live `_startCameraFocus`/`_updateCameraFocus` (WordWeaverScene.js:241-270) instead runs a **fixed 850ms** animation with a **cubic-out ease** (`ease = 1 - (1-t)**3`) via `lerpVectors(start, end, ease)` — a *time-based* model, frame-rate-independent (good), but **not** the spec's exponential-approach factor model, and there's **no 0.01 snap threshold** (it ends on `t >= 1`). `CameraController.js` is a *third* variant (900/800ms `easeInOutCubic`). 5.5.4 reconciles: either adopt the factor-0.08 per-frame lerp the spec/§23.2 name, **or** document the time-based easing as a deliberate, superior-feeling improvement and update §5.6/§23.2 to match — pick one, and add the snap/short-circuit.
- **`OrbitControls` is configured but tuned to the embed, not the spec poses.** Desktop controls exist: `enableDamping` ✓, `maxPolarAngle = π`, `minDistance 0.4`, `maxDistance 80`, `enablePan` ✓ (WordWeaverScene.js:71-77), and the frame loop calls `controls.update()` ✓ (WordWeaverScene.js:744, satisfying §16.1 step 1). Default mouse mapping (left orbit / right pan / wheel zoom) is OrbitControls' built-in ✓ (§5.6 line 570-572). But `maxDistance 80` (and the `_frameYearCamera` bump to 120) **collide with the far plane** (5.5.2) and the ring radius — at `(0,60,120)` overview the camera is 134 units out, beyond both. 5.5.5 reconciles control limits with the corrected far plane and the spec overview distance.
- **Desktop keyboard fly exists but via TWO parallel input paths, with Space/Shift not Q/E, and no R reset.** §5.6 line 573-575 wants WASD fly, **Q/E elevate/descend**, **R reset to overview**. Live reality is split: `WordWeaverTimelineViewport.js` (:46-49) reads `KeyW/S/A/D` **and `KeyQ/KeyE`** (vertical) from its own `window` keydown/keyup listeners **plus** `extForward/extStrafe/extLift`; `WordWeaverChrome.js` (:140-153) **separately** tracks `keysHeld` and maps W/S/A/D + **Space/Shift** (not Q/E) to `scene.setFlightInput(...)` → the viewport's `ext*`. So **two** keyboard handlers feed the same motion (risking double-counting W/A/S/D), they **disagree** on the lift keys (Q/E vs Space/Shift), and **no key resets the camera (R)**. 5.5.6 converges to a single input path, standardizes on Q/E (+ keep Space/Shift as aliases if desired), and adds R → `focusOverview`.
- **Mobile uses `OrbitControls` touch + a custom overlay — partially matching the spec's "custom touch handler".** §5.6 line 577 says mobile uses a **custom** handler (not `OrbitControls`). Live: `OrbitControls` still handles touch orbit/pinch/pan (single class for both platforms), **augmented** by `WordWeaverMobileControls.js` — a forward-slide pad, an analog joystick, and Fly/Descend hold-buttons (WordWeaverMobileControls.js:42-58) whose input is summed into `setFlightInput` via `WordWeaverChrome` (:145-150). This **covers** the spec's thumb-slide-fly and fly/descend buttons (lines 581-583) and, through OrbitControls, single-drag-orbit / pinch-zoom / two-finger-pan (lines 578-580) — but it is **not** the spec's standalone `TouchController` (no `OrbitControls` on mobile). 5.5.7 reconciles: either formalize the overlay+OrbitControls combo as the mobile handler (cheaper, working) **or** build the dedicated `TouchController` — and gate/disable OrbitControls' redundant touch zoom if it fights the pad.
- **Focus API is incomplete, and `focusOnDay` doesn't open the day panel.** §5.6 line 597-602 lists five methods. Live has only **two**: `focusOnMonth` (WordWeaverScene.js:214, no panel ✓) and `focusOnDay` (WordWeaverScene.js:226 — sets `focusedDay`, `setProximity(true)`, animates) but it **does not open the day detail panel** (spec line 598 says it should — or per §5.7 line 613 a *second* click opens it; reconcile the click-vs-focus panel rule with 5.6). **Missing entirely:** `focusOnToday` (line 600, today's m/d), `focusOnNextEvent` (line 601, next upcoming event's date), `focusOnCategory` (line 602, next day with a category event). 5.5.8 adds the three missing methods and reconciles the day-panel trigger.
- **Focus emits no §13.2 events and consumes no `navigateTo` — the recurring Phase-4/5 bus gap.** §13.2 line 1418 says a 3D navigation updates `focusedDate` and emits `dayFocused`/`monthFocused`; line 436 says `WordWeaverScene` should *listen* to `navigateTo`. Live `focusOnDay`/`focusOnMonth` set `_yearLayout.focusedDay` but emit **nothing** on the Phase-2 bus, and the scene listens only to `timelineUpdated` on the *duplicate* WordWeaver bus (the 5.1.9 finding) — so a 2D→3D `navigateTo` won't move the camera, and a 3D focus won't update the 2D views or top bar. 5.5.9 wires focus → emit `dayFocused`/`monthFocused` and a `navigateTo` listener → `focusOnDay`/`focusOnMonth`, on the canonical bus.
- **`prefers-reduced-motion` is not honored — accessibility gap (mirrors 5.4.6).** §23.3 line 1664 mandates that under reduced motion **camera transitions snap immediately** (lerp factor 1 for one frame). `_updateCameraFocus` (WordWeaverScene.js:262-269) always runs the full 850ms eased move with no reduced-motion check (the project *has* a probe — `prefersReducedMotion()` in perfFlags.js:6). 5.5.10 makes focus snap under reduced motion, coordinated with the scene-level motion gate (5.1 / 5.4.6).
- **What's already right (keep):** `PerspectiveCamera` + `OrbitControls` chosen ✓ (§5.6 line 567/569); `enableDamping` ✓ (smooth orbit); aspect-on-resize ✓ (WordWeaverScene.js:634-635); `controls.update()` each frame ✓ (§16.1 step 1, WordWeaverScene.js:744); focus targets read from **world positions** of real layout objects (`getWorldPosition` on cluster/block, WordWeaverScene.js:218/231) ✓; an `_animating`-style guard exists (the standalone `CameraController._animating`, and the inline path single-flights via `_cameraFocus`) ✓; fly-movement moves **both** `camera.position` and `controls.target` together so orbit stays sane after flying (WordWeaverTimelineViewport.js:61-72) ✓; mobile overlay provides accessible large-target fly/descend controls ✓ (§27 touch ergonomics); `controls.dispose()` on teardown ✓ (WordWeaverScene.js:774).

---

## 5.5.1 — Canonical camera subsystem & §11 path reconciliation

- **Purpose:** Resolve the **three parallel camera/movement subsystems** (inline `_cameraFocus` in `WordWeaverScene`, the standalone `CameraController.js` on the 2D wall, the fly-movement in `WordWeaverTimelineViewport.js`) into one canonical 3D-ring camera owner, and reconcile §11 line 1011 (which lists `CameraController` under the scene) with the live inline implementation — so Phase 5 has a single, correctly-located camera authority.
- **Dependencies:** 5.1 (camera + controls are created in scene init), 5.2 (focus targets come from layout objects), 5.6 (the pick path that calls focus); §5.6 line 565, §11 line 1011, §11 line 147.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Exactly **one** camera-transition authority drives the year ring; its location is reconciled with §11 (either extract the inline `_startCameraFocus`/`_updateCameraFocus` into a `CameraController` class as the tree implies, **or** update §11 line 1011 to reflect that the canonical 3D camera logic lives in `WordWeaverScene` — document the decision); the 2D-wall `CameraController.js` is explicitly scoped to `CalendarApp` (a *different* subsystem, not the ring) and not converged away; the fly-movement (`WordWeaverTimelineViewport.js`) is folded into / owned by the canonical camera (5.5.6) so there is one place that mutates `camera.position`/`controls.target`.
- **Implementation notes:** Recommend **extracting** the inline focus logic into a `WordWeaverCameraController` (or reusing/renaming the 2D `CameraController` shape) so the §11 tree is satisfied and the API surface (5.5.8) lives in one class; keep the 2D `CameraController.js` as-is for the wall calendar (it's wired and working — CalendarApp.js:97). Confirm via import graph that the ring uses only the inline path (no `CameraController` import in `WordWeaverScene.js`) and that fly-input currently routes Chrome → `scene.setFlightInput` → viewport (5.5.6 collapses this).
- **Edge cases:** The 2D `CameraController` and a new 3D one must not share mutable state (different cameras/controls); extracting the inline logic must preserve the single-flight guard (`_cameraFocus` non-null blocks a new move mid-animation — or decide moves are interruptible, 5.5.4); the viewport's fly-movement registers its **own** `window` key listeners (WordWeaverTimelineViewport.js:74-77) — folding it in must relocate or dedupe those (5.5.6).
- **UI/UX considerations:** One camera authority keeps transitions, limits, and reset behavior consistent; divergent controllers would drift (e.g. different easing for wall vs ring already exists).
- **Data flow notes:** Pick/nav intent (5.6 / §13.2) → canonical camera API → `camera`/`controls` mutation each frame.
- **Testing notes:** Grep shows one camera-transition owner for the ring; `CameraController.js` is imported only by `CalendarApp`; the §11 path and the on-disk reality agree after the chosen fix; no two code paths mutate `camera.position` in the same frame except the documented fly+focus interplay (5.5.4).
- **Performance notes:** Neutral (organizational); consolidating lets 5.5.4's transition model and 5.5.9's bus wiring land in one place.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Same authority both platforms; input differs (5.5.5/5.5.6 desktop, 5.5.7 mobile).
- **Integration points:** §5.6 line 565, §11 line 1011/147, 5.5.4 (transition model), 5.5.6 (fly fold-in), 5.5.8 (focus API home), `CameraController.js` (wall, kept).
- **Status:** **Divergent (path) + duplicated (3 subsystems).** Ring camera is inline in `WordWeaverScene`; §11 names `CameraController` (which actually drives the 2D wall); fly-movement is a third path in the viewport. **Next:** extract a canonical 3D camera controller (or reconcile §11 to the inline reality), scope the wall `CameraController` separately, fold fly-movement in.

## 5.5.2 — Camera intrinsics to spec (FOV 60°, near 0.1, far 2000, aspect on resize)

- **Purpose:** Reconcile the camera intrinsics to §5.6 line 567 — `PerspectiveCamera`, FOV **60°**, near 0.1, far **2000**, aspect updated on resize — replacing the live `(42, 1, 0.1, 80)`, and **settle the spec's own far-plane disagreement** (line 567 says 2000, line 487 says 1000). The far-plane fix is the direct resolution of the **5.1.4 ring-clip bug**.
- **Dependencies:** 5.1.4 (the far=80 ring-clip finding this fixes), 5.2.1 (ring radius determines the minimum safe far plane), 5.5.3 (overview distance must sit inside the far plane); §5.6 line 567, §5.6 line 487.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Camera FOV is **60°** (or a documented deviation if 42 is deliberately retained for a tighter ring framing — but then §5.6 must be updated); near = 0.1; far is **≥ the spec value and ≥ the farthest visible geometry** at the year-overview pose (`(0,60,120)` overview = ~134 units to origin, so far **must** exceed that plus the back of the ring — 2000 comfortably does, 80 does not); the §5.6 line 487 vs 567 conflict (1000 vs 2000) is resolved to a single number and noted; `camera.aspect`/`updateProjectionMatrix()` on resize is preserved (WordWeaverScene.js:634-635).
- **Implementation notes:** Change `new THREE.PerspectiveCamera(42, 1, 0.1, 80)` (WordWeaverScene.js:53) to FOV 60 / far 2000 (or the settled value); verify nothing else hard-codes far=80 (the OrbitControls `maxDistance 80`/`120` is a *separate* limit — 5.5.5 — not the projection far plane); FOV 60 widens the view — re-check that the year ring still frames well at the corrected overview pose (5.5.3) and that day-focus framing (5.5.3) isn't too wide.
- **Edge cases:** Raising far to 2000 with near 0.1 widens the depth-buffer range — watch for z-fighting on coplanar day-tile surfaces (5.3) at the ring scale (mitigate via `logarithmicDepthBuffer` or a less extreme near/far ratio if it appears); FOV change alters the apparent size of every object — the §5.8 background and atom opacities (5.4) may need a glance; mobile may keep a slightly different FOV via `perfFlags` only if justified (document).
- **UI/UX considerations:** FOV 60 is a natural, non-distorting field; the far-plane fix removes the visible clipping of the ring's far side (the 5.1.4 symptom) — a correctness win the user sees immediately.
- **Data flow notes:** Pure projection config; no model data.
- **Testing notes:** Camera reports FOV 60 / far 2000; at the `(0,60,120)` overview the **entire** ring renders with no far-plane clipping (the 5.1.4 regression test); resize keeps aspect correct; no z-fighting on day tiles at ring scale.
- **Performance notes:** Far-plane size alone is cheap; the depth-precision trade is the only watch-item (mitigations above). FOV 60 may bring slightly more geometry into frustum than FOV 42 — bounded by culling (5.2.9).
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Same intrinsics target; any per-platform FOV tweak is documented and minimal.
- **Integration points:** §5.6 line 567/487, 5.1.4 (ring-clip bug), 5.2.1 (ring radius), 5.5.3 (poses inside far), 5.5.5 (OrbitControls maxDistance is separate).
- **Status:** **Divergent (intrinsics) — contains the 5.1.4 bug.** Live `(42,1,0.1,80)`; spec FOV 60 / far 2000 (line 487 says 1000 — unresolved). **Next:** set FOV 60 / near 0.1 / far 2000, settle the 1000-vs-2000 conflict, fix the ring clip.

## 5.5.3 — Canonical poses: year overview, month focus, day focus

- **Purpose:** Reconcile the three camera poses to §5.6 line 585-588 — year overview `(0, 60, 120)` looking at origin, month focus **~20 units** in front of the cluster, day focus **close-framed** on the `DayBlock3D` — replacing the live `_frameYearCamera` `(0,18,42)`, `focusOnMonth` distance 14, and the **two competing "home" concepts** (`_cameraHome`/`_targetHome` vs `_frameYearCamera`).
- **Dependencies:** 5.5.2 (far plane must contain the overview pose), 5.2 (cluster/block world positions supply month/day targets), 5.5.4 (poses are the lerp endpoints); §5.6 line 585-588, §13.2 line 1101/1278.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Year overview is `(0, 60, 120)` → `(0,0,0)` (or a documented deviation tuned to the *corrected* ring radius — if the ring radius is the live 26 not the spec 80, the overview distance must scale accordingly and the deviation noted, tying to 5.2.1); month focus places the camera **~20 units** out from the selected cluster facing it (live uses 14 — raise to ~20 or justify); day focus frames the block centered and close (the live 5.5/1.2 is in the right spirit — confirm the block fills frame without clipping at FOV 60); the **two home concepts are unified** — one canonical "overview" pose that `_frameYearCamera`, `focusOverview` (5.5.6 R-key / 5.7 Escape), and the embed default all agree on.
- **Implementation notes:** Set `_frameYearCamera` (WordWeaverScene.js:204-208) to `position (0,60,120)`, `target (0,0,0)` (scaled to actual ring radius per 5.2.1); change `focusOnMonth`'s `_startCameraFocus(world, 14, 2.5)` (WordWeaverScene.js:219) distance to ~20; verify `focusOnDay`'s `_startCameraFocus(world, 5.5, 1.2)` (WordWeaverScene.js:233) centers the block at FOV 60; reconcile `_cameraHome`/`_targetHome` (WordWeaverScene.js:57-58, the `(0,2.4,8.2)` embed pose) with the new overview — either make the overview the single home, or clearly label `_cameraHome` as the *embed/satellite* default distinct from the full-screen year overview (the embed may legitimately want a nearer pose — document which context uses which).
- **Edge cases:** If the ring radius stays 26 (5.2.1 unresolved), `(0,60,120)` is far too distant — the overview distance **must** track the ring radius (parameterize, don't hard-code both radius and pose independently); month "~20 units in front" needs a cluster *facing normal* — derive from the cluster's outward direction (the 5.2.2 inside-vs-outside-the-ring vantage question directly affects whether "in front" is toward or away from ring center); day focus must not clip the block at near 0.1 or push it out of frame on mobile's narrower aspect.
- **UI/UX considerations:** A high, far overview `(0,60,120)` gives the "whole year at a glance" read the spec wants; the month pose should make one cluster comfortably dominant; the day pose should make the focused tile legible (handing off to the day panel, 5.5.8).
- **Data flow notes:** Layout world positions (5.2) → pose endpoints → lerp (5.5.4).
- **Testing notes:** Overview matches `(0,60,120)`→origin (scaled to radius); month focus sits ~20 units out facing the cluster; day focus centers the block; `focusOverview`/Escape/R all return to the *same* overview pose; the embed default is clearly the embed's, not double-defined.
- **Performance notes:** Poses are static vectors — negligible; the far-plane interaction is 5.5.2's concern.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Day/month framing may use a slightly larger standoff on mobile's narrower aspect so the target still fills frame (document the per-aspect adjust).
- **Integration points:** §5.6 line 585-588, 5.2.1 (ring radius scales the overview), 5.2.2 (inside/outside vantage = "in front"), 5.5.2 (far contains pose), 5.5.4 (endpoints), 5.5.6/5.7 (reset returns here).
- **Status:** **Divergent (poses) + duplicated (two homes).** Live overview `(0,18,42)`, month distance 14, and a separate `_cameraHome (0,2.4,8.2)`. **Next:** overview `(0,60,120)`→origin (radius-scaled), month ~20 out, confirm day framing at FOV 60, unify the home concept.

## 5.5.4 — Transition model: per-frame lerp factor 0.08 + snap < 0.01 (vs time-based easing)

- **Purpose:** Reconcile the camera-move animation to §5.6 line 590-595 / §23.2 line 1655 — per-frame `camera.position.lerpTo(target, 0.08)` + `controls.target.lerpTo(lookAt, 0.08)` with a **snap when distance < 0.01** — versus the live **fixed-850ms cubic-out** `lerpVectors` (`_startCameraFocus`/`_updateCameraFocus`) and the wall `CameraController`'s 900/800ms `easeInOutCubic`; settle on one model and add the snap/short-circuit and interrupt semantics.
- **Dependencies:** 5.5.1 (single camera authority owns the model), 5.5.3 (the endpoints), 5.5.10 (reduced-motion snaps this); §5.6 line 590-595, §23.2 line 1655.
- **Acceptance criteria:** A single, documented transition model is used for **all** ring camera moves; if the spec's factor-0.08 model is adopted, each frame applies `position.lerp(end, 0.08)` and `controls.target.lerp(end, 0.08)` and **snaps to exact** when `position.distanceTo(end) < 0.01` (then clears the active move); if the time-based easing is **kept** instead (it feels smoother and is frame-rate-independent), §5.6 line 590-595 and §23.2 line 1655 are updated to describe it and a near-end snap/threshold is still added so moves terminate cleanly; **interrupt behavior** is defined (a new `focusOn*` mid-move either re-targets the in-flight lerp from the current pose — preferred for the factor model — or is blocked until the current finishes, the live `_cameraFocus` single-flight); fly-input (5.5.6) during a focus move is reconciled (focus owns the camera until it snaps, or fly cancels focus — document).
- **Implementation notes:** If adopting factor-0.08: replace `_updateCameraFocus`'s `lerpVectors(start, end, ease)` (WordWeaverScene.js:267-268) with `camera.position.lerp(endCam, 0.08)` / `controls.target.lerp(endTarget, 0.08)` and a distance-snap check; the factor model **re-targets naturally** (just change `endCam`/`endTarget`), enabling interruptible focus (drop the `_cameraFocus`-non-null block at :262 in favor of an always-live target). If keeping easing: add `if (distanceTo(end) < 0.01) snap+finish`. Either way, ensure `controls.update()` (WordWeaverScene.js:744) runs **after** the lerp each frame so damping composes correctly, and that fly-movement (which also writes `camera.position`/`controls.target`) doesn't fight an active focus.
- **Edge cases:** The factor-0.08 model is **frame-rate-dependent** (0.08/frame ≈ different speed at 30 vs 120fps) — if adopted, either accept it (spec names it explicitly) or convert to a delta-corrected `1 - (1-0.08)^(dt*60)` for true rate-independence (note the choice); a move whose endpoint equals the start (distance 0) must snap immediately, not divide-by-anything; interrupting a move must not snap-jump; the snap threshold (0.01) is in world units — at ring radius 80 vs 26 it's effectively tighter/looser, but 0.01 is small enough either way.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Factor-0.08 gives the classic ease-out "fast then settle" approach; the live cubic feels similar but has a hard 850ms duration regardless of distance (long hops feel slow, short hops feel laggy) — the factor model's distance-proportional speed is arguably better, which is the case for *either* adopting it or documenting the easing trade.
- **Data flow notes:** Endpoint (5.5.3) + current pose → per-frame interpolation → camera/controls.
- **Testing notes:** A focus move reaches and **snaps** to the exact target (final `distanceTo < 0.01` → equal); a new focus mid-move re-targets without a jump (factor model) or is cleanly queued (easing model); fly + focus don't tug-of-war; visual speed is acceptable at 30/60/120fps (and matches the documented rate model).
- **Performance notes:** Both models are a handful of vector ops/frame — negligible; the win is correct termination (no perpetual tiny lerps) via the snap.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Same model; mobile may accept the simpler factor lerp without delta-correction (document).
- **Integration points:** §5.6 line 590-595, §23.2 line 1655, 5.5.1 (single owner), 5.5.3 (endpoints), 5.5.6 (fly interplay), 5.5.10 (reduced-motion snap).
- **Status:** **Divergent (model) — two easing variants, no snap.** Ring uses fixed-850ms cubic-out `lerpVectors`; wall uses 900/800ms `easeInOutCubic`; neither uses factor-0.08 or a 0.01 snap. **Next:** pick the factor-0.08 per-frame lerp (+ snap, interruptible) **or** formalize the easing and update §5.6/§23.2; add the snap either way.

## 5.5.5 — `OrbitControls` desktop config & limits (reconciled with far plane)

- **Purpose:** Reconcile the desktop `OrbitControls` configuration to §5.6 line 569-572 (left-drag orbit, right-drag pan, wheel zoom, damping) and align its `min/maxDistance` and polar limits with the **corrected far plane (5.5.2)** and the **spec overview distance (5.5.3)** — the live `maxDistance 80`/`120` currently collides with both.
- **Dependencies:** 5.5.2 (far plane), 5.5.3 (overview distance sets the needed max), 5.1 (controls created in scene init); §5.6 line 569-572, §16.1 line 1302.
- **Acceptance criteria:** Left-drag orbit / right-drag pan / wheel zoom work via OrbitControls defaults ✓; `enableDamping` stays on ✓; `minDistance`/`maxDistance` permit the spec poses — at year overview `(0,60,120)` the camera is ~134 units from origin, so `maxDistance` must be **≥ that** (the live 80, even bumped to 120 in `_frameYearCamera`, is too small — the overview would be clamped); `maxPolarAngle` allows the high overhead overview angle; `controls.update()` remains the §16.1-step-1 first frame-loop action (WordWeaverScene.js:744); pan is enabled (or deliberately disabled — the spec lists right-drag pan, so keep `enablePan`).
- **Implementation notes:** Raise `maxDistance` to comfortably exceed the overview distance (e.g. ≥ 160 at the spec ring scale, or radius-scaled per 5.2.1) and reconcile the two places it's set (`71-77` init and `205` `_frameYearCamera`'s `120`); keep `minDistance` small enough for day-focus close framing (live 0.4 ✓ vs day standoff 5.5 — fine); verify `maxPolarAngle = π` (WordWeaverScene.js:74) doesn't let the camera flip under the ring awkwardly (consider a slightly-less-than-π cap if the underside is unintended); ensure the projection far plane (5.5.2) is **larger** than `maxDistance` so zooming to max never clips.
- **Edge cases:** OrbitControls `maxDistance` and the projection `far` are independent — both must accommodate the overview, and `far > maxDistance` to avoid clipping at full zoom-out; damping means the camera keeps gliding a few frames after input — focus moves (5.5.4) must compose with, not fight, residual damping (apply focus, then `controls.update()`); right-drag pan can drift the target far from the ring — `focusOverview`/R (5.5.6) must recenter.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Standard orbit/pan/zoom is the expected desktop 3D interaction; clamped-too-near limits would frustrate users trying to see the whole year (the current symptom).
- **Data flow notes:** Pointer/wheel → OrbitControls → camera; `controls.update()` applies damping each frame.
- **Testing notes:** From overview the user can zoom out to see the full ring (not clamped); orbit/pan/wheel all respond; `controls.update()` is the first per-frame call; max zoom-out never clips the far plane (5.5.2 holds `far > maxDistance`).
- **Performance notes:** Config only; negligible. Damping adds a few settle frames (intended).
- **Mobile vs desktop:** This task is desktop config; mobile touch is 5.5.7 (and may disable OrbitControls touch zoom there).
- **Integration points:** §5.6 line 569-572, §16.1 line 1302, 5.5.2 (far > maxDistance), 5.5.3 (overview distance), 5.5.6 (reset recenters), 5.2.1 (radius-scaled limits).
- **Status:** **Partial — works but mis-limited.** Orbit/pan/zoom/damping ✓, `controls.update()` ✓; but `maxDistance 80`/`120` collides with the far plane and the spec overview distance. **Next:** raise `maxDistance` above the overview distance (radius-scaled), keep `far > maxDistance`, reconcile the two set-sites.

## 5.5.6 — Desktop keyboard: converge the two fly paths, standardize Q/E, add R reset

- **Purpose:** Reconcile desktop keyboard controls to §5.6 line 573-575 — WASD fly, **Q/E elevate/descend**, **R reset to overview** — by **converging the two parallel keyboard input paths** (`WordWeaverTimelineViewport`'s own `window` listeners reading W/S/A/D/Q/E, and `WordWeaverChrome`'s `keysHeld` mapping W/S/A/D + **Space/Shift** into `setFlightInput`) into one, standardizing the lift keys, and adding the missing **R** reset.
- **Dependencies:** 5.5.1 (single camera authority), 5.5.3 (R returns to the canonical overview), 5.5.4 (fly vs focus interplay); §5.6 line 573-575, §5.7 line 624 (Escape return — coordinated with 5.6).
- **Acceptance criteria:** Exactly **one** keyboard handler feeds camera fly (no double-counting of W/A/S/D between the viewport's direct keys and Chrome's `setFlightInput`); lift is standardized on **Q/E** (the viewport already reads `KeyQ/KeyE`, WordWeaverTimelineViewport.js:49 — keep that; Space/Shift may remain as documented aliases); **R** resets the camera to the year overview (`focusOverview`/`_frameYearCamera`, 5.5.3); fly only applies when the 3D canvas has focus and **no text input is focused** (so typing in the Inkling/Writer panels doesn't fly the camera); the handler is owned by the canonical camera/scene (5.5.1), not split across Chrome + viewport.
- **Implementation notes:** Pick one path: either (a) keep `WordWeaverTimelineViewport`'s direct key reading and **remove** Chrome's W/A/S/D→`setFlightInput` (Chrome keeps only what the viewport can't see, e.g. mobile input — WordWeaverChrome.js:145-150), or (b) make Chrome the single source and have the viewport read **only** `ext*` (drop its `window` keydown/keyup at WordWeaverTimelineViewport.js:74-77). Add `R → focusOverview()` and a text-input guard (skip when `document.activeElement` is an input/textarea/contenteditable — the spec's "no text input focused" condition, echoed at §16 keybinding line 1687). Standardize lift on Q/E; if Space/Shift stay, document them as aliases.
- **Edge cases:** Both paths currently summing W means holding W could move at 2× — the convergence must remove the duplicate; key listeners on `window` keep firing when panels are open — the text-input guard prevents WASD hijacking typing (a real bug today); R during an in-flight focus move should re-target to overview (5.5.4 interrupt); released-key cleanup on blur (Alt-Tab away mid-hold) must zero the input so the camera doesn't drift forever.
- **UI/UX considerations:** Q/E for vertical + WASD for planar is the documented fly scheme; R as "take me back to the whole year" is a crucial escape hatch in a free-fly 3D space (pairs with Escape, §5.7).
- **Data flow notes:** Key state → (single) fly input → per-frame `camera.position`/`controls.target` translation (WordWeaverTimelineViewport.js:60-72).
- **Testing notes:** Holding W moves at 1× (not 2×); Q/E change altitude; R returns to overview; typing in a panel input does **not** fly the camera; releasing keys / window blur stops movement; only one keydown handler mutates fly input.
- **Performance notes:** Negligible; removing the duplicate path is a minor correctness/perf win.
- **Mobile vs desktop:** Desktop-only; mobile fly is the overlay (5.5.7) feeding the *same* single fly-input sink.
- **Integration points:** §5.6 line 573-575, §16 line 1687 (keybindings), §5.7 line 624 (Escape), 5.5.1 (owner), 5.5.3 (R target), 5.5.7 (mobile shares the sink).
- **Status:** **Partial + duplicated.** WASD + Q/E exist (viewport) **and** WASD + Space/Shift exist (Chrome → setFlightInput) — two paths, conflicting lift keys, possible double-count, **no R reset**, no text-input guard. **Next:** converge to one handler, standardize Q/E, add R → overview and the input-focus guard.

## 5.5.7 — Mobile touch controls (custom handler vs `OrbitControls` + overlay)
